# Source notes: what we learned from which document

Each entry records an insight, where it came from (document + section/page), and what we
did with it in the simulator. Keep adding entries as documents are read. Local copies of the
documents are in `data/raw/docs/`.

Status key: **applied** (changed the simulator), **pending** (planned change), **context**
(informs explanations or the assumptions panel only), **conflict** (contradicts something
we assumed; see the note).

---

## Eric (project owner), conversation, 2026-10-31

| Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|
| Almost every Berkeley Hills street is two lanes (one each direction) with parking on both sides. | **applied**: `lanes.hillsLocal = 2`, `parking.hills = "both"` in `data/assumptions.json`, labeled assumption. Later refined to "both sides where it fits" so a travel lane always remains. |
| 2026-11-01: on many hill streets with parking on both sides, cars meeting from opposite directions must take turns. | **applied 2026-11-01**: two-way streets with one lane (or less) between parked cars now share one lane; when both directions have traffic, drivers take turns and the street loses capacity (`sharedLane`). Returning commuters now drive in against the evacuation. |
| 2026-11-01: the Oakland Hills and Kensington have the same narrow, steep streets, and a fire will not stop at the city line. | **applied 2026-11-01** (X1–X11): OpenStreetMap streets for Oakland, Emeryville, Albany, Kensington, El Cerrito and Richmond inside the elevation grid, joined to the County network at the city line; about 74,700 residents outside Berkeley placed from Genasys estimates or 2020 Census blocks. The hand list of hill streets continuing out of the city is gone. North Kensington (about 1,500 people) lies outside the grid. |
| 2026-11-01: many Berkeley Fire Department members live outside Berkeley. In a large regional disaster, crews called in from home could be delayed reaching the city, so the department may be short-staffed until they arrive. Not sure whether it is possible or valuable to model. | **pending**: design note written 2026-11-01, `docs/design/fire-staffing.md` (FS1–FS12). No public source says where members live; the only Berkeley callback time on record is 68 minutes in 1991 (FS5), while the simulator's results are mostly settled in the first hour, when on-duty staffing decides. A scratch test shows which stations are available changes engine reach a lot (FS12). Recommended first step: a setting for companies committed at ignition, used only for engine reach. |
| 2026-11-01: don't wait on the City. It is slow with data requests, and a California Public Records Act (CPRA) request takes months. Unless a dataset is critical, go without it and disclose the error it causes. Asking Berkeley Fire informally which hill streets engines struggle on is reasonable. | **applied 2026-11-01**: signal timing comes out of the data request; the simulator now discloses the gap with error bounds (Assumptions & limits, "Information gaps"; the results panel shows the Time Study's 90% time for the same region and time of day). The formal letter is reduced to an informal question to Berkeley Fire. |
| 2026-11-01: for OKL-003 (Claremont Canyon, in the R04 Panoramic Hill order list) use the 2020 Census count (307) instead of the Genasys estimate (27). Resolves conflict X6. | **applied 2026-11-01**: `population.overrides` in `data/assumptions.json` (labeled assumption, citing Eric and the Census blocks); the builder replaces the zone's population with the Census count and marks it census2020. |
| 2026-11-01: open The Crossways (County SEGID 181035806, 67 m, Overlook Rd to Middlefield Rd) as a street. The County record and the City segment's network fields code it pedestrian or stairway; OpenStreetMap, the City pavement records and KLD's AB 747 model treat it as a street. Resolves conflict K8. | **applied 2026-11-01**: second entry in `network.segmentCorrections` in `data/assumptions.json` (TRVLWAY NT → B, OSM way 6391261), its `why` naming the sources on each side; the inspector shows the County value and that reason. Effects in K8 and the calibration recheck below. |
| 2026-11-01: for BER-013 (Panoramic Hill, the part in Oakland, an R04 order zone) use the 2020 Census count (122) instead of the Genasys estimate (83). Resolves conflict Z2. | **applied 2026-11-01**: `population.overrides` in `data/assumptions.json`, as for OKL-003 (labeled assumption, citing Eric and the Census blocks in the zone's Genasys outline); the inspector shows 122 as census2020 with the Genasys estimate beside it. Effects in Z2 and the calibration recheck below. |
| 2026-11-01: surprised that the parked cars and engines comparison shows little difference between A and B; the summary says B is better, but the table shows more people still there when embers arrived under B at low severity. | **applied 2026-11-01**: checked 15 runs (east wind, S6, seeds 1–5, three severities): people still there when embers arrived differ by −20 to +61 between plans, all of it on the road and none at home, with no consistent sign; engine travel time to the fire edge is identical in every run, though B halves the segments too narrow for an engine (284 → 143). Cause: engines do not fight the fire in this simulator, and the engine's route to the fire edge uses streets that stay passable. Parking still changes hill-street traffic a little (Codex, PR #8: eastWind low S6 seed 1, 281.10 → 286.99, all on roads), so the texts say the tested options made no consistent difference rather than that parking cannot change exposure. Codex also required every seed to have a value for both plans before a difference is called consistent (a missing 90% time no longer counts as agreement). The comparison table now colors a difference only when it points the same way in every run (gray otherwise); the summary says when people still there show no clear difference; the parking question, intro and Assumptions & limits state the limit. **Applied 2026-11-01** (ER1–ER3): the engine measure is built, "Hill homes an engine can reach within 5 minutes (City standard)", and the parking question now headlines hill residents an engine cannot reach within 5 minutes (about 1,500 → 1,000 central, fewer under B in 15 of 15 pairs on every fire). **Pending**: suppression effects. |
| 2026-11-01: in a real evacuation, police will take over the traffic signals. | **applied 2026-11-01** (CF1–CF14): Expert-view plan setting "Police control of junctions" (off by default): officers from a pool of 10, 20, 40 or 80 (Berkeley Police staffing for scale, CF7) arrive 15, 30 or 60 min after the first order and take over the City's 44 pre-timed signals, optionally also the Time Study's Appendix F congestion points or every signal, busiest first; a controlled junction passes 9% more vehicles and serves outbound approaches first (assumption, CF8). Still a **conflict** with the Time Study, which keeps signals on their normal timing and uses manned control only "where it is advisable or where existing plans call for" it (§4.3, p. 4-7), so calibration stays with the setting off. Berkeley Police cannot staff all 146 signals at once; the default pool is 20. |
| 2026-11-01: a two-lane road can be repurposed as two lanes in one direction (contraflow). | **applied 2026-11-01** (CF3, CF4, CF12, CF13): Expert-view plan setting "Contraflow" on the main hill exit streets or streets the user picks. Both lanes run outbound only where at least 20 ft stays clear between parked cars; precedent: Bille Rd, a two-lane road, ran both lanes out in the 2018 Camp Fire (NIST TN 2252, p. 101). |
| 2026-11-01: signs that announce "contraflow mode" could be triggered as part of a rehearsed egress plan; electronic signs, though a fire could knock them out, and Red Flag days can bring PG&E Public Safety Power Shutoffs. | **applied 2026-11-01** (CF5, CF6, CF9–CF11): contraflow can start with pre-installed signs and gates (10 min after the order, no officers, capital cost per entry point) on grid power, battery backup or a mechanical fallback; a sign fails if the fire reaches it first or, on grid power, in a power shutoff, and falls back to officers or to no contraflow. New Scenario setting "Red Flag power shutoff" (off by default) also darkens signals, which then run as all-way stops. Rehearsal making people leave sooner is not modeled (CF15). |
| 2026-11-01: "We should build this option." | **applied 2026-11-01**: built as the three settings above, all off by default, with baseline runs identical to main (calibration note below); robustness on the three Simple-view fires in CF14. |
| 2026-11-01 (UI feedback): change the default playback speed to 15 (min/s). | **applied 2026-11-01**: playback starts at 15 min of simulated time per second (a 4-hour run plays in about 16 s); 1 and 5 min/s stay available. |
| 2026-11-01 (UI feedback): when running a scenario it is not obvious when it is complete; Pause and Play again require scrolling while Simple view's Watch buttons do not. | **applied 2026-11-01** (PR #7): the map clock shows progress and turns green with "Run complete"; the results heading and a status line say the run has ended; Play/Pause and Restart sit beside the map clock. |
| 2026-11-01 (UI feedback): Compare A and B takes more than half a minute; "a few minutes" is better. The running and "#/# simulations" progress belongs in a small banner above the map. | **applied 2026-11-01** (PR #7): Compare's text says it takes a few minutes; its progress shows in a banner at the top of the map with a progress bar instead of in the side panels. |
| 2026-11-01: add an "R0" zoning option: no work that would increase population, such as ADUs or additions to existing homes. | **applied 2026-11-01**: Future growth (Scenario: none, middle housing 870 units, moderate 2,706, maximum ADU build-out 27,060) and R0 (plan: no added units in the Leave Early Zones, Fire Zones 2–3 or access-constrained areas), with its legal status shown (L1–L9) and results under "Future growth and R0". Additions that add floor area but no unit add no household, so R0 acts on added units only. Original note: Scope (Eric, 2026-11-01): R0 blocks new units only; it does not count housing lost to fire as people moving out. Framing (Eric, 2026-11-01): state law is not a hard limit on the option, since Berkeley could ask the state to reconsider; show R0 with its legal status (what current law allows, and what would need a state change), not as ruled out. needs a growth scenario to compare against, since the simulator holds today's population. Plan: future-growth settings from the Time Study's ADU and middle-housing figures (E26) allocated by area, and an R0 setting that freezes growth in a chosen area (Fire Zones 2–3, Leave Early Zones, or the access-constrained areas from the choke-point map). Legal feasibility under California ADU law, and Berkeley's current hillside ADU rules, to be researched from public sources. |
| 2026-11-01 photo of Bret Harte Rd: parking on one side only (the downhill-right side, partly on the dirt edge); the other side is fence and hedge with no room to park. One car-width moving lane remains; gaps between parked cars (driveways) are the passing spots. | **context**: agrees with the City Narrow_Roads survey for Link 1379 (17 ft pavement, 10 ft with parking = one side parked). The network builder labels that segment "both sides", but the simulator uses the survey's 10 ft clear width, so the result is right. Shows parking is often one-sided where a bank or fence lines the road, so "both sides" overstates parking there. Gap spacing is evidence for the passing-spot rule. |
| 2026-11-01 photo of Poppy Ln: parked on both sides; the right-side car sits partly on a gravel shoulder off the pavement. About one car-width moving lane between them; take turns. | **context, possible conflict**: City Narrow_Roads (Links 1878, 1937, 2183) gives 22 ft pavement and **8 ft** with parking, so the simulator blocks fire engines on all of Poppy Ln (needs 10 ft). The photo looks wider than 8 ft, perhaps because cars park partly off the pavement, but a photo can't settle 8 ft vs 10 ft. Needs a tape measure or BFD's view. Supports "both sides" here, unlike Bret Harte Rd. |
| 2026-11-01: one bad parking job narrows the gap. A single car parked far from the curb, or one wide vehicle, sets the clear width for the whole block. | **applied 2026-11-01**: the narrowest point governs, not the typical width. Checked the City Narrow_Roads layer: pavement minus width-with-parking is exactly 7 ft (56 segments), 14 ft (246) or 16 ft (52) on 354 of 356 segments, so it is **computed** (7 or 8 ft per parked side), not measured, and assumes perfect parking. Each run now draws bad parking jobs whose chance grows with the number of parked cars on a segment (`parking.range`); they apply to engine access and to the lanes that fit. Plan option "enforce parking tight to the curb" removes them. |
| 2026-11-01 photo of Miller Ave toward Latham Ln: curbs both sides, dashed yellow centerline, cars parked on the right side only. The left curb has bins and no parked cars. | **conflict**: the City survey gives 21 ft pavement and **7 ft** with parking (both sides) on all 13 Miller Ave segments, so the simulator blocks engines on the whole street. With one side parked it would be about 14 ft: one take-turns lane, and engines pass. |
| 2026-11-01 photo of Latham Ln: no centerline. One car is parked on the right at the curb by a driveway. The car on the left is parked entirely on a dirt shoulder, off the pavement. | **conflict**: the survey gives 21 ft and 7 ft with parking (both sides) on both Latham segments. Cars on an unpaved shoulder take no pavement, so here the clear width looks like 14 ft or more. A single daytime photo can't show evening parking. |
| 2026-11-01 photo, street unknown (hills, curbs, dashed yellow centerline): a full-size cargo van parked a foot or more off the curb. Its left side reaches almost to the centerline. Another car is parked on the far side further up. | **context → pending**: an example of a bad parking job made of two parts. (1) Vehicle width: a full-size van is about 6.5–7 ft wide without mirrors, so it already uses most of a 7–8 ft per-side allowance. (2) Gap from the curb. At this spot the street drops to one moving lane even though it is striped for two. Model: per-car draws of vehicle width (share of vans, pickups, SUVs) plus curb offset. |
| 2026-11-01 photo of Avenida Dr: cars parked tight to the curb on one side only (downhill right). The other side is curb and hedge with no parking. One moving lane. | **context**: Avenida is not in the Narrow_Roads survey. City pavement width is 18–23 ft. The simulator's "both sides where it fits" rule drops to one side here (18 ft − 8 = 10 ft, just enough for an engine), which matches the photo. A single wide parking job would block engines. **Bug found:** before a run, the map's engine layer uses the network builder's "both sides" values and shows Avenida, Fairlawn and Arcade as blocked; after a run it shows the sim's "where it fits" values. These should agree. |
| 2026-11-01 overhead image (Fairlawn Dr / Arcade Ave / Grizzly Peak Blvd), from a commercial map service: parked cars visible along much of each street, mostly on one side, with driveway gaps between them. Tree canopy hides parts of the street. | **context**: overhead imagery shows which side is parked and where the passing gaps are along whole blocks. Street-level photos are better for curb offset and vehicle width. For anything systematic, use imagery whose licence allows deriving data (public orthoimagery), not commercial map screenshots. House numbers in the image are not recorded. |
| 2026-11-01: the City's orthophotos (2020 and 2017 at 3 in pixels) are public on `gis.cityofberkeley.info` (Imagery folder). Tested an export near Avenida Dr: individual parked cars, driveways and curb lines are clearly visible. USGS National Map has only lidar here, no high-resolution imagery. | **applied 2026-11-01** (see City aerial counts below): preferred source for systematic parking counts (side parked, cars per block, passing gaps) instead of commercial map screenshots. Daytime, single date. Public City imagery (Eric, 2026-11-01: no permission needed); only derived counts are kept in the repo, to keep it small. |
| Pattern across the four photos (Bret Harte, Poppy, Miller, Latham): the survey assumes the same per-side deduction everywhere. In practice parking is often on one side, or partly or wholly off the pavement, and sometimes one car parked badly sets a narrower gap. | **applied 2026-11-01**: survey "width with parking" is now the both-sides case. Model parking per segment as uncertain (sides parked, how far onto the pavement, chance of a bad job), with the survey and "both sides" as the pessimistic end. Field counts or curb data narrow the range. |
| 2026-11-01: proposed piloting "egress navigation": routing people out efficiently using GPS and real-time constraints with traffic flow, not just navigation. | **applied 2026-11-01** (DG16–DG24): plan setting Driver information and routing (Expert view, off by default) with Today's model, Habit, Ordinary app, Constrained egress guidance and Unconstrained system-optimal (comparison only), a share of drivers who follow, and a household-by-household "households worse off" measure in the results panel and Compare. |
| 2026-11-01, same conversation: "huge liability in routing based on system optimal since you may end up killing some people." | **applied 2026-11-01** (decision: build it the constrained way): guidance offers a load-balancing route only if, for that driver, it is no riskier under the fire forecast and at most 60 s slower than the driver's own best route, never routes toward the fire or forecast ember areas when another way out exists, and never tells anyone to delay leaving (DG17). Unconstrained system-optimal routing is shown only to measure what the constraint costs or saves, labeled as able to send some households on riskier routes and not a recommended design. Compare flags any routing option that leaves households worse off. |

## City of Berkeley, "Evacuation StoryMaps: Text-Only Version", July 2025

URL: https://berkeleyca.gov/sites/default/files/documents/Evacuation%20StoryMaps%20Text-Only%20Version%20July%202025.pdf
(still live 2026-10-31; local copy `data/raw/docs/Evacuation StoryMaps Text-Only Version July 2025.pdf`).
It summarizes the 2025 Evacuation Time Study by KLD. KLD drove every street and surveyed the community; the field observations are from May 2023.

| # | Insight (section) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Evacuation times are **longer** on weekdays, in daytime, and when school is in session, because more people are in Berkeley and there is more traffic both in and outside the evacuating area. Times are shorter at night and on weekends. (§1.2) | **conflict → pending**: our 5 PM case *reduced* demand (70% of residents home). Replace it with daytime population added to residents. |
| S2 | ETE ranges. 1923 Fire Repeat: 1 h 35 min – 4 h 10 min. Panoramic Hill Fire: 45 min – 1 h 45 min. Tsunami Max Phase: 1 h 50 min – 2 h 15 min. Tsunami Phase 3 (west of I-80): 55 min – 1 h 45 min. (§1.3) | **pending**: calibration targets for `tests/`. |
| S3 | 1923 Repeat ETE by season and time. Spring/Fall/Winter: midweek daytime 4 h 10 min, weekend daytime 1 h 45 min, evening 1 h 35 min. Summer: midweek daytime 2 h 25 min, weekend daytime 1 h 50 min, evening 1 h 35 min. The clock starts at the evacuation order. (§3.3) | **pending**: time-of-day presets and calibration. |
| S4 | 1923 Repeat order area: Berkeley's eastern edge west to Sacramento St, Marin Ave south to the UC campus. Warnings go to surrounding areas. (§1.3, §3.3) | **pending**: use the City's `Evac_Regions` R02/R03 polygons (already downloaded) instead of our distance rule for this scenario. |
| S5 | Evacuation = notification → mobilization → roadway time. (§2.1) | **context**: matches our alert delay + preparation + driving model. |
| S6 | 1923 Repeat household example: leave at 30 min → safe at about 1 h 10 min; leave at 1 h → 2 h 15 min; leave at 1.5 h → 3 h 10 min; leave at 2.5 h → 3 h 50 min. Waiting 30 more minutes can add over an hour. (§2.2) | **pending**: calibration target for congestion growth. |
| S7 | 1923 Repeat roads: mostly clear at the start, 50% full after 30–45 min, completely jammed after 120 min. (§2.2) | **pending**: calibration target. |
| S8 | Highest-capacity routes: University Ave, Adeline St, Ashby Ave (SR 13), Cedar St, I-80, San Pablo Ave (SR 123), Sacramento St, Claremont Ave, Marin Ave. They are not enough alone; evacuees must use all streets. (§2.3, §6.6) | **context**: we already allow all streets. The list can be shown in the inspector or legend. |
| S9 | The study gives times only for people leaving by car. Many residents have no car; the City says to carpool and not to rely on government transport (buses can't drive through fire; vehicles may be scarce or blocked). Officials do not recommend walking or biking out of the hills in a wildfire. (§2.4–2.5) | **context**, supports our "no vehicle = at risk" display. Phase 2 should model carpooling and neighbor help before buses. |
| S10 | 1923 fire history: a grass fire entered the built area around 2:00 PM, pushed by Diablo winds of 25–40 mph. It spread SW until about 4:20 PM, when moist westerlies took over, reaching Shattuck Ave and Berkeley Way. It moved through about **1.3 km of buildings in 2.5 h**. (§3.2) | **pending**: 1923 calibration. That is ~9 m/min through buildings; our central rate was faster. |
| S11 | Present-day shift: the edge of neighborhoods is now 1.3 km farther uphill. The study moves the fire's entry point 1.3 km uphill: about 2.5 h to Codornices Park / Rose Garden, then 2.5 h more to Shattuck, so **about 5 h** from the eastern edge to Shattuck. (§3.2) | **pending**: move our 1923 ignition to today's eastern edge and tune the central spread to about 5 h. |
| S12 | Combined 1923 run (fall midweek daytime, ETE 4 h 10 min): at 1 h the fire reaches the eastern border and congestion extends into the flats; at 2 h fire is spreading over roads in use; the evacuation is complete at 5 h; fire reaches Shattuck at 6 h. Some evacuees would be overtaken on roads. (§3.3) | **pending**: qualitative check for our playback. |
| S13 | Parking restrictions won't get evacuees out faster. Intersections are the chokepoints, and where roads narrow cars still go one by one. Selective restrictions on wider streets may help engines get in; engines are not caught in the same gridlock because most traffic is outbound. (§4.1) | **applied** (independently): the parking note and the model mechanism. Now cited. |
| S14 | Traffic calming (diverters, circles, humps) and pre-timed signals make evacuation traffic worse. Areas called out: S and W of UC campus, S and W of Clark Kerr, N of Ohlone Park, LBNL. (§4.2) | **pending**: an intersection-capacity penalty, and a possible "signal retiming / calming removal" intervention. |
| S15 | SB 99: 139 Primary Access Impaired Neighborhoods (one way in and out; 1,547 residential parcels) and 195 Secondary AINs (only one viable exit because of narrow roads and hairpins; 3,715 parcels, mostly in the North Berkeley Hills). Panoramic Hill has the most primary-AIN parcels, with a single exit at a stop-controlled intersection (Panoramic Way & Prospect St/Canyon Rd). (§5) | **pending**: aggregate the City parcel layer's `Single_Access` / `Narrow_Rd` / `Tight_Turn` flags by street segment, without addresses. |
| S16 | Narrow roads are rated constrained, moderately constrained or severely constrained. They are almost all north of Codornices Park and on Panoramic Hill. (§5) | **context**: our Narrow_Roads layer uses MINOR/MAJOR; look for the 3-level rating in the SB 99 report. |
| S17 | AB 747 capacity from May 2023 field observations, in four bands: 225–900, 901–1,700, 1,701–5,700 and 5,701–15,750 passenger cars/h. Hills: mostly ≤ 900 pc/h, free flow ≤ 20 mph. Most city streets are single-lane collectors, ≤ 45 mph, about ≤ 1,700 pc/h. (§6.1) | **pending**: hills values are consistent with ours (700/lane at 20 mph). Flats are higher than our 800–900/lane, so raise them. |
| S18 | AB 747 safety rating (fatality exposure, width, turns, slope, pavement, bridges/tunnels); least safe are the hills and Panoramic Way. There is also a separate hazard rating and a demographic vulnerability index (four levels; highest in Southside, campus, around Downtown BART, and the Berkeley portion of Panoramic Hill). (§6.3–6.5) | **context**: candidate map layers if the report has data. |
| S19 | **Fire pace in Simple view (Eric, 2026-11-02: "There's a fine line between alarmist and complacency. Perhaps 'simple' mode needs one lever to pull that lets people see the more serious case?").** Check (scratch script, seeds 1–5, farthest burned cell downwind of the ignition at 64 min): medium severity reaches a median 807–975 m by fire (range 517–1,399); high reaches 1,767–2,184 m (range 1,551–2,474), i.e. 1.1–1.4 miles. 1991: "Just 64 minutes after the fire broke out, it was burning in the Rockridge district, more than a mile downwind from the point of origin" (USFA-TR-060, PDF p. 55; FS15). So Simple view labels medium "Like 1923 (the City's repeat)" (its calibration, S11) and high "Like 1991"; low stays in Expert view. Default 1923. The control changes the watched run only: comparisons already run low, medium and high (15 passes). | **applied**: `js/main.js` PACE, setPace (localStorage `bhe-pace`). The 1991 match is in downwind distance at 64 min only; the model's fire is not calibrated to 1991's spread through houses. Fire crews (Eric, 2026-11-02: saying firefighting "isn't modeled" "makes it sound like we are assuming that no engagement exists at all"): the page says the paces come from real fires and so include the firefighting effort seen in them, that crews focus on evacuation in a wind-driven fire (FS14), and that the simulator does not add crews holding the flanks or the fire stopping when the wind changes (FS15), so "the scenario may exaggerate the spread as winds die and firefighters establish control" (Eric's wording). |

## 2025 Evacuation Time Study (KLD), SB 99 report, AB 747 report

The City URLs linked from the StoryMap returned 404 on 2026-10-31. Copies were retrieved
from the Wayback Machine:

- Evacuation Time Study: capture 2025-07-16 (identical to 2026-02-01)
- SB 99 Access Impaired Neighborhood Analysis: capture 2025-07-19
- AB 747 Evacuation Route Safety, Capacity, and Viability Analysis: capture 2025-07-19

### SB 99 Access Impaired Neighborhood Analysis (KLD TR-1380, Rev. 3, PDF dated 2024-02-19, 23 pp.)

Local copy: `data/raw/docs/Access Impaired Neighborhood Analysis - SB 99 Report.pdf`

| # | Insight (page) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | Parked cars: **8 ft per parked side** (16 ft for both sides), using the **City's curb data** for where parking is allowed. 8 ft was chosen over 7.5 ft because cars are not always parked tight to the curb and large vehicles park on streets. Overhanging vegetation is not counted. (p. 2–3, fn. 8) | **pending**: we use 2.1 m (6.9 ft). Change to 2.44 m (8 ft). The "City's curb data" is a dataset we don't have yet; add it to DATA_INVENTORY. |
| A2 | Minimum viable evacuation travel lane is **10 ft**. Set with City Public Works and Fire staff: 8 ft vehicles plus mirrors. (p. 2–3, fn. 7) | **pending**: our "one moving lane" threshold is 2.75 m (9 ft). Change to 3.05 m (10 ft). |
| A3 | Largest City fire truck (ladder truck): **11.25 ft high, 60 ft long, 9.25 ft wide**. (p. 2, fn. 6) | **pending**: replaces our 3.0 m engine-clearance assumption with a sourced 9.25 ft body width. Note that a 10 ft lane is the practical minimum. |
| A4 | Narrow-road tiers by clear width after parking: **severely constrained ≤ 6 ft, moderately 7–8 ft, constrained 9–10 ft**. Roads ≥ 10 ft are not narrow. (p. 4) | **pending**: use these tiers in the clear-width street coloring. The City `Narrow_Roads` layer is this analysis's output. Its `Width_w_Parking` appears to subtract 7 ft per side, not 8 (Hill Rd 14 → 7; Contra Costa Ave 18 → 4). Check this. |
| A5 | Tight turns: circumcircle radius of 3 consecutive centerline vertices; **< 81 ft** (AASHTO 2018 minimum for a 20 mph design speed) flagged. Surveyed curve speeds were ≤ 20 mph. (p. 3–4, fn. 10) | **context**: explains the City `Tight_Turns` layer. We could compute it for all segments. |
| A6 | Fire station service area = **5-minute drive** (from the City's Standards of Coverage). Parcels outside it are more vulnerable. (p. 2) | **applied**: the results compare engine travel time to the fire edge with 5 minutes (2026-10-31), and since 2026-11-01 show the share of hill residents an engine can reach within 5 minutes (`access.engineServiceMin`; ER1). |
| A7 | Results: **141 primary AINs (1,461 parcels)** and **195 secondary AINs (3,687 parcels)**. Rev. 3 numbers differ slightly from the StoryMap's 139 / 1,547 and 195 / 3,715. Zones E013 and E058 (Panoramic) have the most AIN parcels. There is one access point, a stop-controlled intersection at Panoramic Way & Prospect St/Canyon Rd. Upper barriers there would not help because they don't reach Prospect/Canyon. Most of the neighborhood is beyond a 5-min engine drive. **Many roads there are < 10 ft and have no street parking.** (p. 4) | **conflict → pending**: our default "parking both sides where it fits" is wrong for Panoramic. The Narrow_Roads values already override it there; check other non-narrow-layer segments. |
| A8 | Dead ends were checked with aerial imagery. Man-made barriers (bollards, gates, calming islands) are flagged as "potential emergency exits". (p. 2–3) | **pending**: our County network does not mark diverters or bollards, so we treat those as open through-routes. Needs a barrier layer. |

### AB 747 Evacuation Route Safety, Capacity, and Viability Analysis (KLD, Rev. 3, PDF dated 2024-03-07, 61 pp.)

Local copy: `data/raw/docs/Evacuation Route Safety, Capacity, and Viability Analysis - AB 747 Report.pdf`

| # | Insight (page) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | Capacity is computed per road section from the May 2023 field survey (lanes, free-flow speed) using HCM 2022. Intersections and ramp junctions are **not** included in these section capacities. (p. 5) | **context**: our separate intersection caps remain a model rule until the Time Study gives values. |
| B2 | **Two-lane road capacity by speed (Table 2):** 5 mph → 225, 10 → 450, 15 → 675, 20 → 900, 25 → 1,125, 30 → 1,350, 35 → 1,575, 40+ → 1,700 pc/h. That is 45 pc/h per mph up to 1,700. Free-flow speeds include the effect of traffic-control devices. (p. 6, p. 14) | **pending**: replace our class-based per-lane capacities with this rule applied to segment speed. Hills at ≤ 20 mph → ≤ 900. |
| B3 | Multilane roadways: **1,900 pc/h per lane** (conservative, HCM Exh. 12-8). Freeways: **2,250 pc/h per lane** (55 mph). (p. 6) | **pending**: raise arterial (900 → 1,900/lane) and freeway (1,900 → 2,250) capacities. |
| B4 | Demand sources: 2020 Census extrapolated to April 2023; LEHD/OnTheMap commuting employees; UC Berkeley 2019–20 enrollment; HPMS 2020 I-80 through traffic. Employees and commuting students: 1 per vehicle. Transit and tourists are not included. Off-campus students are assumed to live in Berkeley. No remote-work reduction. (p. 2–4) | **pending**: template for our daytime population (fixes conflict S1). |
| B5 | Temporal variation: employees peak 9 AM–5 PM weekdays; **only about 10% of employees present on weekends and evenings**. Minimum demand = UC not in session; maximum = in session. (p. 8) | **pending**: time-of-day presets. |
| B6 | *(Superseded by E9.)* **Mobilization curve (Fig. 12)**, digitized by eye from the figure, minutes after the order. **Residents:** 0% at 5 min, ~10% at 37, ~20% at 50, ~40% at 68, ~50% at 77, ~60% at 88, ~80% at 110, ~90% at 128, ~97% at 155, ~100% at 210. **Employees/tourists:** 0% at 5 min, ~17% at 10, ~42% at 15, ~61% at 20, ~75% at 25, ~93% at 35, ~99% at 50, 100% at 60. The peak hourly share for residents is **57%, from 45 to 105 min**. The curve comes from an online survey of residents and employees. (p. 8, p. 28) | **conflict → pending**: our notice + preparation median was about 28 min; the survey-based median is about 77 min. Replace our lognormal values with this curve, sampled piecewise. |
| B7 | Vulnerability inputs, per census tract (ACS 2017–21): poverty, households without vehicles, Caltrans H+T affordability, disability by type. (p. 3) | **pending**: ACS vehicle availability by tract can replace our flat 20% "no vehicle" assumption. |
| B8 | Viability scores: connectivity (shortest paths from block-group centroids to city exits, e.g. I-80 EB at Buchanan exit 13A and I-80 WB at SR 13/Ashby), accessibility (number of connections), services (gas/charging drive time), congestion (v/c). (p. 4, p. 7–8) | **context**: names the city exits KLD used. |

### Evacuation Time Study (KLD Engineering, PDF dated 2025-06-23, 323 pp.)

Local copy: `data/raw/docs/Evacuation Time Study.pdf` (~225 MB, Wayback capture 2025-07-16). Text extract: `data/raw/docs/ets.txt`.
Read by a subagent on 2026-10-31; page labels are the report's (e.g. 5-14). Model used: DYNEV II, a macroscopic dynamic traffic assignment model.

**Scope and regions**

| # | Insight (page) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | 116,762 residents (2020 Census projected to Dec 2024) in **106 evacuation zones = the Genasys Protect zones**. 14 regions × 6 scenarios = 84 cases. (ES-1, 3-14, 6-2) | **context**: our zone layer is the same Genasys set. |
| E2 | **R02 1923 Fire** Order zones: BER-E012, 013, 015–020, 026–033, 038–043, 057–059; LBL-E001–006; OKL-E001, E002. Warning zones: BER-E007–010, 021, 022, 025, 037, 044, 054–056, 060, 066, 073; OKL-E004; ALB-E006. R02 uses the fire's *final* perimeter. (6-6, 7-5) | **pending → applying**: use these zone lists instead of the region polygon. |
| E3 | **R04 Panoramic Hill** Order: OAK-E003–005, BER-E013, 058, 059, 066. Warning: OAK-E006, 007, 009; BER-E041, 055, 057, 060, 073, 077. The eastern part lies in Oakland, but its residents must drive out through Berkeley. (6-7, 3-2) | **pending → applying**. Our County street file has no Oakland streets, so OAK zones' population is dropped. |
| E4 | R03 phased 1923 orders (a → b → c, east to west, "based on historical fire spread data"). Phasing adds about +2 h 15 min to the 90th-percentile ETE because of a mobilization spike and inbound cross-traffic; the study recommends ordering all zones at once. **Internal inconsistency:** phase timing is +90/+180 min in most places but +30/+60 min in Table 6-3 and §2.2. (5-8, 6-9, 7-6, 7-7) | **pending**: a candidate plan option "phased orders". |

**Demand and behavior**

| # | Insight (page) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| E5 | **2.37 persons per household** (ACS; survey 2.39). Footnote D-2 says 2.61 was used, which contradicts §3. (2-1, 3-3, D-2) | **applying**: replaces our 2.3. |
| E6 | Evacuating vehicles: **1.14 per household** (survey, 1,453 households). Fig. D-11: 0 vehicles 5.9%, **1 = 73.6%**, 2 = 14.6%, 3 = 1.3%, 4+ = 0.5%, bike 1.4%, foot 2.2%, bus 0.5%. (2-2, D-11) | **applying**: replaces our 1.0–1.6 range (central 1.3). |
| E7 | **7.47% of households have no vehicle** (ACS, Table 3-10). **86.9% of carless people rideshare**, leaving 1,686 people (1.4%) who need buses. (3-9, 3-22) | **applying**: replaces our 20%. Carless households now ride with neighbors at that rate; the rest need transport (Phase 2). |
| E8 | Compliance: **100% in Order zones, 50% in Warning zones, 20% voluntary/shadow** in other city zones and the Shadow Region (from NUREG/CR-7002). The study notes real shadow evacuation is not uniform. 0.8% would ignore an order, but the model assumes 100% compliance. (2-3, 7-1, D-4) | **applying**. |
| E9 | **Mobilization histograms** (Table 5-9, p. 5-14), % of trips started per 15-min period after the order. **A** employees/visitors 22, 38, 33, 7. **C** residents with a returning commuter 0, 0, 3, 7, 13, 17, 19, 17, 12, 7, 3, 1, 1. **D** residents without one 4, 12, 18, 22, 20, 14, 7, 2, 1. **E** = C plus child pickup 0, 0, 0, 2, 6, 10, 16, 17, 17, 14, 9, 5, 3, 1. **F** = D plus child pickup 0, 3, 9, 16, 20, 20, 15, 10, 5, 1, 1. Shadow evacuees use C. Mobilization starts within 15 min of the order; the ETE clock starts at the order. (5-14, 2-2) | **applying**: **supersedes** the AB 747 Fig. 12 curve (B6). D's median is about 52 min; the AB 747 resident curve's median was about 77 min. |
| E10 | Notification component: 37% by 5 min, 41% by 10, 46% by 15, 71% by 20, 93% by 25, 100% by 35. Prepare-home component: 15.3% by 15 min, 57.7% by 45, 92.1% by 75, 100% by 135. (5-10 to 5-12) | **context**: already folded into the E9 histograms. |
| E11 | Six scenarios (Table 6-5): S1 summer midweek midday, S2 summer weekend midday, S3 summer night, S4 **fall midweek midday**, S5 fall weekend midday, S6 **fall night**. Households with a returning commuter: **13% midweek, 1% otherwise**. Employees 96–100% midweek, 10% otherwise. College 100% in fall. School buses 100% in S4, 10% in S1, 0% otherwise. External traffic 100% by day, 40% at night. **Residents are not reduced in any scenario.** (6-11) | **conflict resolved → applying**: our "70% home at 5 PM" was wrong. Replace our two time-of-day presets with S1–S6. |
| E12 | Non-resident daytime demand: 25,341 employees (15,256 vehicles), visitors 13,823 vehicles, college 15,188 vehicles, schools 16,179 people. Through traffic **47,962 vehicles** on CA-24, I-880, I-580 and I-80, stopping 2 h after the order (−60% at night). Background fill about 5,545 vehicles. Total R01 S4: 150,994 vehicles. (3-11, 3-12, 3-23) | **pending**: needs locations for employees, visitors and students. Not yet in the simulator; flagged in the assumptions panel. |
| E13 | Leave-early sensitivity: in R02, 10,330 of about 27,830 evacuating vehicles are in Fire Zones 2–3. Leave-early rates of 10/50/75% remove 3.7/19/28% of R02 demand; the model assumes **half of early leavers relocate within the Flats** (shadow demand). 90th-percentile change: −3/−14/−14% with normal mobilization; up to −40% with urgent mobilization. (10-3, 10-14) | **applying**: plan options become 10/50/75%. *Pending*: half of leavers relocating to the Flats. |

**Capacity and network**

| # | Insight (page) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| E14 | Two-lane roads **1,700 pc/h per lane** (two-way max 3,200), reduced for steep grades, tight curves and no shoulders. Multilane 1,900–2,300. Freeway 2,250. (1-5, 4-5, 4-6) | **context**: consistent with B2/B3 (AB 747 expresses the reduction through speed). |
| E15 | Signals: Q = (3600/h)·((G−L)/C)·P with L = 5 s lost time per phase. Signals with detectors are actuated; others pre-timed with City timings. Stop/yield and roundabouts modeled explicitly. Link capacity = the lesser of section and intersection capacity. (4-3 to 4-7) | **pending**: our per-node intersection caps are still a model rule. |
| E16 | **Traffic circles about 900 veh/h per lane; diverters are disconnected links; speed humps reduce speed.** (G-1) | **pending**: needs a diverter/circle layer (the City has diverters; gtpb lists Milvia/Hearst, Virginia/Grant, McGee/Cedar, California/Rose). |
| E17 | Capacity drop: discharge falls to **0.90 × capacity** after breakdown. Exit links with downstream signals are reduced 25%. (4-4, 4-8) | **pending**. |
| E18 | All roads are usable; the study advises **against designated routes and route signage**. Highest-capacity exit routes: I-580, San Pablo, Sacramento, MLK, Adeline/Shattuck, Telegraph, College, Arlington, Grizzly Peak, Ashby. Feeders: Solano, Marin, Hopkins, Durant, Oxford, Spruce, Bancroft, Haste, Cedar, University, Dwight, Euclid. (9-1 to 9-3) | **context**. |
| E19 | Parking restrictions were **not simulated quantitatively**. The finding that red-curbing is "unlikely to substantially improve evacuation flow" is qualitative: intersections are the bottleneck, hill geometry already caps speeds, and most parked cars will themselves leave. Targeted restrictions might help responder ingress. (9-4, 11-8) | **context**: our model's parking result is a mechanism, not a calibrated finding; say so in the parking note. |
| E20 | DYNEV does **not** model responder ingress, buses against the flow, pedestrians or bikes (1-7). It does not model behavior around active fire (2-2). | **context**: our responder layer goes beyond the study, so it stays a model rule. |

With urgent mobilization, the R04 90th percentile goes from 0:30–1:00 to 0:30–0:50 (at most −33%). (10-4, 10-16) | **benchmark 2026-11-01** (EG2, EG8): the City's "2nd Route Out" layer is this connection; `tools/egress-sim.mjs` compares it with the candidates in `docs/egress-candidates.md`. Still **pending** as a Phase 2 intervention. |
| E26 | **ADUs:** units assumed at 2.37 persons and about 1.14 vehicles each. Maximum ADU/JADU build-out is 9,020 parcels × 3 = 27,060 units (64,132 people, 30,848 vehicles), giving 90th-percentile ETE **R02 +102% (S4 3:20 → 5:15), R04 +52%, R05 +154%**. Middle housing: 870 units, at most +8%. (10-5 to 10-8, 10-17 to 10-21) | **applied 2026-11-01**: Future growth settings (`growth` in `data/assumptions.json`); the spec asks for uncertain ranges per unit, while the study uses fixed base rates, so the simulator uses the study's 2.37 persons and its vehicle shares (about 1.12 vehicles per unit). **Correction:** +102%, +52% and +154% are the study's averages over its six scenarios, measured against its 2031 Future Case (Table 10-14 text, p. 10-6); fall weekday (S4) alone is +58% for R02 (3:20 → 5:15), +25% for R04 (2:00 → 2:30) and +97% for R05 (2:45 → 5:25) (Table 10-12). |
| E27 | Converting pre-timed signals to actuated: up to −13% (90th percentile). (10-9, 10-22) | **pending**: Phase 2 intervention. |

| E29 | Transit-dependent and AFN needs: bus mobilization 90 min for schools and medical, 180 min for transit-dependent routes. Loading 1 min per ambulatory passenger, 5 per wheelchair, 30 per bedridden. R04 needs 13 buses, 28 wheelchair vans and 27 ambulances. 6% of households need outside help (1.6% wheelchair van). emPOWER: 348 people. **No age breakdown; stairs not mentioned.** (2-6, 2-7, 3-11, 8-2 to 8-7, D-2) | **pending**: Phase 2 assistance model. Stairs remain unsourced. |
| E30 | Recommendations include: Traffic Control Points at the App. F congestion locations; cutting through traffic; school pickup outside the zone; keeping the Leave Early policy; stricter ADU rules in the Hillside Overlay; carpooling, with commuters not returning; removable traffic calming; actuated signals with battery backup and pre-emption; targeted parking restrictions for responders. (11-1 to 11-15) | **context**: candidate interventions for Phase 2. |
| E31 | **No fire-spread modeling** anywhere in the study. "Since the fire origin, travel direction and rate of spread is not modeled, Claremont Avenue was modeled as a viable evacuation route." Overlaying fire spread is recommended future work. (F-4, 11-1) | **context**: the StoryMap's fire timeline (S10–S12) comes from separate City work not in these reports. Our fire overlay remains a model rule with only the StoryMap for calibration. |

**Conflicts between documents**

- Mobilization: the AB 747 Fig. 12 resident curve (median about 77 min) vs. ETS Table 5-9 D (median about 52 min) and C (median about 100 min). The ETS is the later, final study, so we use the ETS tables.
- AIN counts: the StoryMap says 139 / 1,547 and 195 / 3,715; SB 99 Rev. 3 says 141 / 1,461 and 195 / 3,687.
- Household size: 2.37 in §3 vs 2.61 in footnote D-2.
- R03 phase timing: +90/+180 min vs +30/+60 min.
- ADU effect on R02: the study gives +102% as the average over six scenarios (p. 10-6; by scenario +58% to +123%, Table 10-12), while Berkeley Ord. 7,983-N.S. findings say it "could increase evacuation times by as much as 102%" (L8, as quoted by our research pass). Text typos in §10.5: "43,1464" people (43,146) and "19,0754" units (19,075).

## City of Berkeley 2020 orthophotos (3 in), parking counted on 22 hill blocks, 2026-11-01

Counts: `data/field/aerial-parking-counts-2020.json` (derived; the imagery is not stored, to keep the repo small). Five streets Eric photographed plus 17 random two-way Local hill segments, 70–220 m. One daytime moment in 2020; tree canopy hid 10–70% of each block; counted by Claude, not yet checked by a person.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | On blocks of 22 ft or less, 7 of 9 were parked on one side only or sparsely, 1 had none, 1 (Poppy Ln) had both sides on part of the block. | **applied**: matches the rule that residents stop parking where under 8 ft would remain; no extra parameter needed. |
| C2 | On wider blocks (24–34 ft), of 12 readable: 2 had no parked cars, 4 one side or sparse, 6 both sides on at least part (Scenic Av and Spruce St both sides along most of the block). | **applied**: `noParkingShareHills` 0.15, `oneSideShareHills` 0.35, both sides 0.50. |
| C3 | About one parked car per 19 m of a parked side (daytime, corrected for hidden canopy). Gaps between cars, driveways included, are frequent. | **applied**: `curbMPerCar` 15 (fuller evenings assumed). Gap spacing supports `passingGapM` 40 m. |
| C4 | Curb offset, vehicle width and off-pavement parking cannot be judged at 15 cm per pixel from directly above. | **context**: bad parking jobs and off-pavement shares stay photo-based placeholders. Evening street-level photos or a tape-measure survey would replace them. |
| C5 | The County centerline is offset by up to about 5 m from the pavement on some curving hill streets (Bret Harte Rd, Poppy Ln). | **context**: matters for snapping future curb or imagery data to segments. |

Resolves the "Leave Early Zones" gap in DATA_INVENTORY. |
| W2 | Checking those IDs exposed two bugs in our zone layer: (1) the layer's `zonenum` field is not unique across cities (Albany's and Oakland's zone 6 were also labeled "BER-06"), so some households were assigned to the wrong zone's order time; (2) five Berkeley zones (E013, E023, E035, E047, E050) are split into 2–5 features that each carry the full population, adding about 10,000 people. | **fixed**: zones are keyed and merged by `zone_id` and labeled as the City shows them (BER-006). Berkeley zone population now totals 121,759. The zone descriptions now match Eric's screenshots (BER-007 Woodmont Av, BER-058 Panoramic Hill). |



Files: `data/raw/osm/berkeley-barriers.json` (mirror, data as of 2026-05-31) and `data/raw/osm/barriers-on-roads.json` (overpass-api.de, data as of 2026-11-01). Bounding box 37.835–37.91 N, 122.31–122.23 W (includes parts of Oakland, Albany, Kensington). © OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL. Query suggested by Eric.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| O1 | In the box: 670 gates, 227 bollards, 107 roundabout ways (traffic circles), 466 humps, 130 bumps, 83 dips, 67 tables, 3 mapped diverters, 36 chokers/chicanes. No `mini_roundabout` nodes. Most gates and many bollards are on driveways or service roads. | **context** |
| O2 | 61 barrier points sit on public streets (not service roads). **41 are within 15 m of a street the simulator treats as open**, mostly Berkeley's flats diverters (Milvia, Ellsworth, Dana, Webster, Kelsey, Etna, McKinley, Delaware, Grant …). 5 are in Fire Zones 2–3: bollards on Garber St (motor_vehicle=no) and Tanglewood Rd (motor_vehicle=no, emergency=yes), Napa Av (emergency=yes), Marin Av (bollard access=yes, probably not a closure; gate access=permissive). | **applied 2026-11-01**: 19 barriers on public streets matched our network within 15 m; 18 close their segment to residents' cars (engines pass at gates and emergency=yes bollards after a 3-min delay). Bollards tagged access=yes are traffic-calming islands and stay open. Tanglewood Rd stays open because closing it would cut streets off in our network, which stops at the city line. The bollard tagged on Kelsey St matched a Derby St segment, probably the Derby St diverter the Time Study names (E24). Still to check against the City's diverter list. **Superseded 2026-11-01 (O6)**: the City list is now the list of record; OSM barriers close only a street of the same name, and the block is split at the barrier instead of closed whole. |
| O3 | Panoramic Way has a gate; South Park Dr (Tilden) has a gate (seasonal closure); Derby St diverter (Time Study E24) is not among the barrier points, so it may be mapped as missing road geometry or not mapped. | **applied 2026-11-01**: the Panoramic Way gate closes upper Panoramic Way (O3b); the Derby St diverter is a diagonal split at Piedmont Av (O3a). The South Park Dr gate in Tilden closes South Park Dr at the gate (engines pass); its seasonal opening is not modeled. |
| O2a | The bollard behind our Derby St closure (node 9681832594, `access:delivery=no`) sits on Southwest Place (way 128467230), 9 m north of Derby St, not on Kelsey St. The City's traffic-calming layer lists it as KLD-added #329, closing Southwest Place. Our build closed Derby St from Warring St to South St (edge 1887, 176 m), which is on the Panoramic Hill exit route in the Time Study (F-4), and blocked engines there too. (Overpass via maps.mail.ru, 2026-11-01) | **applied 2026-11-01**: Derby St between Warring St and South St is open again. A barrier now closes only a street of the same name; the Southwest Place bollard matches no street in the network (Southwest Pl is not in it) and closes nothing. City #329 tests open on Derby St. |
| O3a | The Derby St diverter is at Piedmont Ave and Derby St (Time Study G-4; City layer #44). OSM maps it as a diagonal split of the intersection (Derby west joins Piedmont north; Derby east joins Piedmont south; cycleways 24620186 and 1452456159 cross the gap), with no barrier node and no turn restriction, so our build missed it. Piedmont Ave and Parker St (#296) is mapped the same way. Our network treats both as open 4-way intersections. | **applied 2026-11-01**: the builder splits each junction by OSM connectivity. #44 Piedmont Av / Derby St: Piedmont north + Derby west, Piedmont south + Derby east (OSM pairing confirmed by drive tests on OSM ways). #296 Piedmont Av / Parker St: the same pairing. Engines pass both after 3 min (OSM tags a crossing cycleway or bollard emergency=yes within 30 m, e.g. Derby St cycleway 24620186). Derby St traffic heading west from Panoramic Hill now turns south on Piedmont Av (see the calibration recheck). |
| O3b | The Panoramic Way gate is node 35719248 on upper Panoramic Way (way 6399396), in Oakland and 504 m beyond our network, tagged `barrier=gate, vehicle=no, motorcar=no, bicycle=yes, foot=yes`. It separates Panoramic Way from the Stonewall–Panoramic Trail. The pasted claim that it is tagged `access=emergency` is wrong. | **applied 2026-11-01**: the gate splits Panoramic Way; the 335 m beyond it ends in a dead end, so that piece is closed to residents' cars (no households placed on it); engines pass after 3 min. |
| O6 | Our OSM build closes 10 of the City's 75 diverters correctly (Milvia, Harmon, Napa, 63rd, Dana, Ellsworth at Ashby and at Dwight, Webster, Kelsey, Etna). About 30 more appear in OSM as short cycleway gaps that the County centerlines keep open, including Russell St at Piedmont Ave (#33), Avalon Ave at Claremont Ave (#292, named in the Time Study) and Forest Ave at College Ave (#295, one-way out). None of the 18 closures is a partial diverter, but each closes a whole block (80–290 m) even when the barrier is 8–32 m from one end. | **applied 2026-11-01**: every City diverter is a site; the builder matches each street arm to OSM about 40 m out (both carriageways of a divided street) and tests which arms OSM lets a car drive between. Of the 75: 32 closures (Russell St east of Piedmont Av #33, Avalon Av at Claremont Av #292), 13 junction splits (#44, #296), 17 one-way pieces of 15 m (Forest Av at College Av #295, out only), and 13 open: 6 with no restriction in OSM (#56, #60, #256, #268, #275, #329), 1 with banned turns only (#303, not modeled), 1 not matched in OSM (#67), 1 with no junction or street within 25 m (#253), 4 undone because they would cut streets off (#258) or leave cars on one-way streets with no way out (#59, #272, #283). Mid-block barriers split the block, so each half stays reachable. Engines pass 20 of the 45 closures and splits. 9 OSM barriers away from the City points also close streets (Carleton St, Garber St, Panoramic Way, South Park Dr, Page St, University Dr, Camelia St, Spyglass Hill, Binnacle Hill); the University Av "block" (on an unnamed way) and the Buchanan St "fence" (on Gilman St) no longer close anything. Per-device results: `network.meta.barrierSites`; on the edge: `barrier.cityId`, `barrier.action`. The City's 63 circles join the OSM roundabouts for the circle capacity (127 junction nodes). |
| O7 | 4 of our 18 closures (Garber St 3182, 10th St 3274, Channing Way 2220, Jones St 2083) sit within 13 m of a node our network already treats as a dead end, so closing the edge cuts the block off. The cut-off check does not catch this. | **applied 2026-11-01**: a barrier within 20 m of a dead end is skipped (Channing Wy, Jones St, Tenth St, Devon Wy, Tres Mesas: "nextToDeadEnd" in `meta.barrierSites`). Garber St's end node now joins Oakland streets, so its bollard closes Garber St at the barrier. |
| O8 | OSM traffic signals: 242 `highway=traffic_signals` nodes inside Berkeley (Overpass, 2026-11-01), against 146 in the City layer (G4); OSM counts some intersections more than once. None on Arlington Ave, Spruce St, Euclid Ave or Grizzly Peak Blvd. OSM does not say whether a signal is pre-timed. The pasted claim of `highway=bus_trap` or planter tags at Derby St is wrong (no bus_trap nodes in Berkeley; all 11 planters are downtown). | **context**: use the City layer for signals. |
| O5 | OSM parking and width tags in Fire Zones 2–3 (113 km of drivable ways, Overpass via maps.mail.ru, 2026-11-01): parking tags on 4 ways (0.23 km), `width` on 1, no `parking:lane:*`. `lanes` covers 63%; two-way streets tagged `lanes=1` have a median City width of 7.3 m, those tagged `lanes=2` 9.8 m. Hill Ct (way 6347383) is tagged `parking:both=no`, against our default of both sides parked; 15 City no-parking signs within 120 m support OSM. The `berkeley-barriers.json` extract came from a mirror whose data dates from 2026-05-31. | **context**: too sparse to use; keep City widths. Hill Ct is a test case for using the City sign layers. Closes HANDOFF item 11. |
| O4 | Traffic circles as `junction=roundabout` ways, including the small residential circles. | **applied 2026-11-01**: 86 network nodes inside OSM roundabout rings capped at 900 veh/h (E16). |
| O5 | OSM parking-lane and width tags in the hills: not checked yet (Overpass servers at capacity). | **pending**: retry later; likely sparse. |

## Streets and people beyond Berkeley: OpenStreetMap, Genasys and the 2020 Census, 2026-11-01

Files: `data/raw/osm/east-bay-roads.json` (overpass-api.de, retrieved 2026-11-01 15:35 UTC, OSM data as of 15:31 UTC; query in `east-bay-roads.overpassql`; overpass.kumi.systems and maps.mail.ru returned 504), `data/raw/genasys/zones.json` (the WFS behind protect.genasys.com, layer `zonehavenv2:evacuation_zone`, 188 zones in the box), `data/raw/census/blocks-2020.json` (TIGERweb 2020 blocks: population, housing units and interior points, no outlines) and `kensington-cdp-2020.geojson`. Box 37.836–37.912 N, 122.334–122.206 W. © OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| X1 | OSM has 5,822 drivable ways in the box (residential 2,896, tertiary 1,215, secondary 769, primary 395, motorway and links 415). Tags: `lanes` on 3,409 ways, `maxspeed` on 3,874, `oneway` on 2,610, `width` on 18, parking tags on about 50. | **applied**: streets outside Berkeley come from OSM; inside Berkeley the County file stays. Lanes and maxspeed from tags where present (`lanesSrc = osm:lanes`); widths are an assumption, the median City width of Berkeley streets of the same class in the hills or the flats (`widthSrc = assumption:berkeleyMedian(...)`), so parking and take-turns rules apply there too. |
| X2 | Joining the two networks: 140 OSM street ends meet the County network (122 at a County dead end); 173 OSM streets are cut at the edge of the elevation grid. Problems found on the way: Claremont Av runs along the city line near Tanglewood Rd and is missing from the County file; the County file stops I-80 at the Ashby Av ramps, short of the Emeryville line; split carriageways (Tunnel Rd, I-80) joined the wrong County end until one-way direction was checked. | **applied** in `tools/build-network.mjs` step 3b: OSM is kept within 40 m inside the city line where no County street is within 8 m; freeway pieces are kept unless a County freeway is within 25 m; joins check one-way direction and road kind (freeway to freeway). Five one-way OSM pieces that still ended in a dead end (failed joins; a 13 m El Camino Real stub trapped one R05 car) are dropped. 38 County dead ends within 100 m of the line stay dead ends; checked by hand, they are courts and stubs (Roslyn Ct, Eton Ct, Panoramic Pl, Ajax Pl) or streets the OSM network also stops (Albany line at Posen, Albina, Acton; Summit Rd). |
| X3 | With Oakland's streets in, the Tanglewood Rd bollard (motor_vehicle=no, emergency=yes) no longer cuts streets off: upper Tanglewood Rd reaches Claremont Av through Oakland. | **applied**: the bollard now closes its segment, like the other 31 OSM closures (32 in all; 10 of them on OSM streets, e.g. gates on Spyglass Hill, Binnacle Hill, Caldecott Ln, South Park Dr). The keep-open rule stays as a guard at the edge of the model area; it now keeps nothing open. |
| X4 | The City layer's Oakland, Albany and Emeryville zones are slivers along the city line that carry the whole zone's population: OKL-E021 (Bushrod, 6,173 people) covers 2,400 m² in the City layer and 939,000 m² in Genasys (233 acres in the City layer's `acreage` field). | **fixed**: outlines for every non-Berkeley zone come from the Genasys WFS; the City layer's estimates are kept for the 26 zones it has. Before this, those zones' people were scaled by the sliver's share inside Berkeley, so almost all were dropped. |
| X5 | The Genasys WFS carries no population. For the 59 zones in the model area that have no City estimate, the 2020 Census count of the zone's blocks (by block interior point) stands in. Where both exist the two agree within about 15% in most zones (ALB-005 2,482 vs 2,436; OKL-020 3,455 vs 3,593; Berkeley as a whole 121,759 vs 121,211). | **applied**: `pop`, `popSrc` (genasys or census2020) and `census2020` on every zone in `layers.json`. |
| X6 | **Conflict:** OKL-003 (Claremont Canyon, an R04 Panoramic Hill order zone) has 27 people by the Genasys estimate and 307 by the 2020 Census blocks inside it. OKL-005 (also R04) has 0 in the Census. | **applied 2026-11-01 (Eric)**: OKL-003 uses the 2020 Census count (307) through a labeled override, `population.overrides` in `data/assumptions.json`; the inspector shows the zone's population as census2020 with the Genasys estimate beside it. OKL-005 keeps Genasys. Effect on R04 in the calibration recheck below. |
| X7 | Kensington CDP, 2020 Census: 5,428 people, 2,334 housing units (TIGERweb Places layer 26, GEOID 0638086). It spans 37.898–37.919 N; the elevation grid stops at about 37.91 N. 3,934 Kensington residents live inside the grid, 4,941 inside the downloaded box. Genasys splits Kensington across El Cerrito's zones (ECK-007 "Kensington - South", ECK-008 "Kensington - (Southeast)" and parts of others); the City of El Cerrito publishes the zones for both. | **applied**: Kensington streets inside the grid are modeled and their residents placed from Census blocks. The north part of Kensington (about 1,500 people) is outside the model area: extending it would mean refetching the DEM farther north (`tools/fetch-dem.mjs`; the 3DEP tiles cover it). |
| X8 | Population now placed by city (inside the model area): Berkeley 121,759 (unchanged); Oakland 31,734 (of 40,637 in the zones touching the area); Albany 21,406; Emeryville 4,434; El Cerrito and Kensington (ECK zones) 12,350; Richmond 4,463; Contra Costa unincorporated and Orinda zones about 290. | **applied**: about 74,700 more residents. All of them can be ordered by the plan's distance rule, and 20% of those not ordered leave as shadow evacuees. |
| X9 | The Time Study models a Shadow Region beyond Berkeley (I-580, Piedmont Av and Moraga Av to the south; SR-13 and SR-24 to the southeast; Tilden Park to the east; Moeser Ln, San Pablo Av, Potrero Av and S 55th St to the north): 114,671 residents, 20% of whom evacuate (Time Study §3.2, p. 3-4; study assumptions 8 and 10, p. 2-3). | **applied**: shadow evacuation now covers everyone in the model area outside the order and warning zones. The model area holds the part of the Shadow Region between about 37.84 N and 37.91 N: about 74,700 residents outside Berkeley, against the study's 114,671 (the model area also takes in a few zones east of SR-13 that the study leaves out). Shadow evacuees now drive out of the model area instead of to the city line (the study's destinations lie beyond its Shadow Region). |
| X10 | Hills outside Berkeley: the CAL FIRE LRA hazard layer in the City's FHSZ service rates Hiller Highlands and Claremont Canyon "Moderate" and Upper Rockridge "NonWildland" at the points checked, which does not match their history (1991 Tunnel Fire). | **applied (model rule)**: outside Berkeley, a street counts as a hill street (one lane each way, hill speeds and parking) when its midpoint is at 90 m elevation or higher, the threshold `js/fire.js` already uses for hill residential land outside the city. 900 OSM street segments qualify. The CAL FIRE layer is not used. |
| X11 | Time Study through traffic (Table 3-12) includes CA-24 SB, 6,930 vehicles over 2 h. CA-24 from the Caldecott Tunnel west is now in the network. | **applied**: `nonResident.through["CA24 SB"]`, loaded on the westbound CA-24 carriageways. I-580 and I-880 stay outside the model area. |

## Pasted notes on egress modeling (source unknown, shared by Eric 2026-11-01)

Treated as suggestions, not evidence: no citations, and some claims could not be checked.

| # | Suggestion | Our assessment / use |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Model bollards, gates and diverters as impassable for cars; removable bollards and mountable curbs let engines through. | **applied** (see O2). |
| P2 | Emergency gates (e.g. to East Bay Regional Parks) take 2–5 min to open. | **applied as an assumption**: `access.barrierDelayS` 180 s for engines at gates and removable bollards. |
| P3 | Night (10 PM–6 AM) parking is fullest; apply the largest width reductions then. | **applied as an assumption**: `parking.range.night` for S3/S6 (fewer empty blocks, one car per 9 m). Hill length closed to engines rises from 10–12 km by day to 15–16 km at night under status-quo parking. |
| P4 | Use official designated evacuation routes as the base network. | **conflict**: the Time Study advises against designated routes and route signage (E18); the simulator keeps all streets usable. |
| P5 | UC Berkeley ITS has published Berkeley/Oakland Hills evacuation models with Network Analyst files on eScholarship. | **unverified**: worth a search; no specific publication named. |
| P6 | Parknav or City Residential Permit Parking data for curb occupancy. | **context**: Parknav is commercial; RPP zones govern permits, not physical space, and mostly cover the flats. |
| P7 | "28 ft streets reduced to under 14 ft" by parked cars. | **consistent**: 28 ft − 2 × 8 ft = 12 ft in our model; median hill pavement is 24 ft. |

## Public City sources checked against an unsourced claim list, 2026-11-01

Eric pasted an AI-written list of public sources for the four data-request items. A research pass checked each claim against the City's ArcGIS organization, its open data portal, Council packets, the Time Study and the 2024 Local Hazard Mitigation Plan (LHMP). Raw downloads were kept outside the repo.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | The City's open data portal (data.cityofberkeley.info, 65 assets) has no residential permit parking (RPP), street-sweeping or curb-parking data. RPP is in the City's ArcGIS org as 17 area polygons and address eligibility (`Public_Online_RPP_Map_WFL1`, edited 2026-08-19), not by block face. Its parcel sublayers include owner names; do not download them. | **context**: no parking-side data here. The pasted claim was wrong. |
| G2 | Street sweeping (`Street_Sweeping_Map_WFL1`, edited 2023-10-31): of about 1,000 segments in Fire Zones 2–3, 771 are `mech_sweep = excluded`. Side of street is recorded only for the 47 weekly-swept segments, 2 of them in Fire Zones 2–3. | **context**: sweeping data says nothing about hill parking. |
| G3 | The City centerline field `PARKING` (in our `street_centerline_city.geojson`) is 1 on 146 segments and 2 on 62, almost all in Fire Zones 2–3. "2" includes Panoramic Wy, Arden Rd, La Loma Ave and Miller Ave; "1" includes Cragmont Ave (20 segments), Shasta Rd, Keeler Ave and Hilldale Ave. The codes are undocumented. | **pending**: test what the codes mean against our aerial counts and Eric's photos before using them. Eric's Miller Ave photo shows parking on one side, so "2 = no parking" does not fit there. |
| G4 | City layer `Signals` (services1.arcgis.com/IYiCpZoSIq9lAxi8, `Signals/FeatureServer/0`, created 2025-06-06): 146 signals, field `ControlTyp` 102 actuated and 44 pre-timed (counts rechecked 2026-11-01). Its pre-timed set matches the Time Study's App. G list (Cedar St at Sacramento, MLK, Shattuck and Oxford; Haste St at Dana, Ellsworth and Fulton; La Loma/Hearst), so it is very likely the study's signal-control input. It has no timings. The Time Study says "detailed signal timings were provided by the City of Berkeley" (p. 1-5). | **pending**: use control type per junction (E15, E27). Timings stay an information gap (disclosed in the simulator). |
| G5 | Hill-edge signals in G4: none on Arlington Ave; The Alameda/Marin pre-timed, Colusa/Marin actuated; Tunnel/Oak Ridge actuated; Claremont/Ashby actuated; Domingo/Ashby, College/Ashby, Claremont Ave/The Uplands and Piedmont/Dwight pre-timed. The study names no adaptive signals and recommends them ("Many existing signals in the city are pre-timed and lack flexibility", Rec. 5, p. 11-7). It uses manned traffic control only "where it is advisable or where existing plans call for" it (§4.3, p. 4-7). | **conflict**: the pasted claims that hill signals are mostly actuated or adaptive and that the study assumed police override are wrong. The report is KLD TR-1296, Rev. 0, July 1, 2025, by KLD Associates, Inc., using DYNEV II. |
| G6 | City layer `Berkeley_Fire_Apparatus_Route_Map_WFL1`, layer 1 "Existing Traffic Calming Devices" (edited 2025-10-28): 331 points, of which 75 diverters, 63 circles, 184 humps, 8 tables and 1 gate (2nd St/Virginia St). Four are marked "Added by KLD". The Derby St diverter of E24 is #44 at Piedmont Ave/Derby St (−122.2513, 37.8622); the Time Study places it there (G-4: "The diverter at Piedmont Ave and Derby St prevents traffic from traveling westbound"). The KLD-added point near Derby St and Warring St (#329) closes Southwest Place, not Derby St (O2a). The list has no Panoramic Way gate. Layer 3 gives apparatus-route counts per street. | **applied 2026-11-01** (O6): saved as `data/raw/city/traffic_calming.geojson` with `.meta.json` (`tools/fetch-city-layers.mjs traffic_calming`); the list of record for diverters, the gate and circles. Gate #304 (2nd St / Virginia St) closes 2nd St north of Virginia St (engines pass). |
| G7 | The City's published narrow-streets list is Attachment 1 to Council Item 28 of Dec 3, 2019 (Disaster and Fire Safety Commission, Safe Passages), reprinted in Item C Supplemental 2 of Dec 10, 2019: 261 street segments with pavement under 26 ft (231,679 ft; Planning, Oct 2015; packet pp. 9–16), with width and Fire Code class only. The packet names Tamalpais Rd as a documented access problem (p. 4). Council items of Sept 13, 2022 (Item 22, p. 2) and Feb 11, 2025 (Special Item 1d, pp. 1–3) ask staff to identify pinch points; no resulting list was found. | **applied 2026-11-01**: Tamalpais Rd added to the question for Berkeley Fire. The widths duplicate `PAV_WIDTH_RD`. |
| G8 | Berkeley Fire has 7 stations and nine fire companies at 3 per company, 120 firefighters in all (Council Item 49, 2024-05-07, p. 3). A fourth firefighter rides Engine 3 permanently from mid-2025 and Engines 4 and 7 in wildfire periods, at about $3,600 a day (Special Item 1c, 2025-02-11, p. 4). Ambulances carry 2 paramedics (FY26 mid-biennial Fire presentation, 2025-05-22, slide 6). No public source states a daily on-duty total. Our arithmetic gives about 36 (27 on companies, 8 on ambulances, 1 battalion chief). The 2023 Citygate Standards of Cover is cited but not posted. | **pending**: staffing design note written (`docs/design/fire-staffing.md`); the daily total is derived, not sourced. Refined by FS1 from the labor agreement: 37 to about 45 on duty. The pasted figure of 35–37 has no source. |
| G9 | Vacancies are "backfilled by float personnel first, then voluntary overtime, then forced/mandatory overtime" (FY26 Fire presentation, slide 7). Mutual-aid hours ranged from 838 to 8,576 a year, FY16–FY24 (slide 4). The 2024 LHMP says after a Hayward earthquake "Outside fire departments may not be able to provide mutual aid" (p. B-51) and describes no off-duty recall. The Alameda County Operational Area Emergency Operations Plan was not found online. | **pending**: no public callback time standard (the MOU allows immediate recall, FS3); a staffing layer would have to state the delay as an assumption. See `docs/design/fire-staffing.md`. |
| G10 | Genasys Protect's public map shows zones, alert polygons, road closures, evacuation points and fires (help.genasys.com, "Genasys Protect FAQs"). The FAQ does not mention routes or gates. Berkeley's live layers could not be viewed without running the app. | **context**. |

Task from Eric, 2026-11-01, after the access-constraints analysis (PR #3, not merged; its row AC3) found that the County file closes the link from the foot of Panoramic Way to Prospect St. Files: `data/raw/street-centerlines.geojson` (County), `data/raw/osm/east-bay-roads.json` plus a fresh Overpass query of every way in 37.8680–37.8705 N, 122.2525–122.2490 W (overpass-api.de, OSM data as of 2026-11-01 17:16 UTC), `data/raw/city/street_centerline_city`, `narrow_roads`, `street_centerlines_fire` and `no_parking_fz23` (the last also carries the City's AB 747 street attributes), `data/raw/docs/sb99.txt`, `data/raw/docs/ets.txt`.

|---|---|---|

It joins Prospect Street (ways 1091777312, 659099707), Panoramic Way (1226669336) and Canyon Road (1375154399). **Bancroft Steps is a separate feature**: footways and steps 311861379–311861382 and 24025452 (`foot=yes`, `motor_vehicle=no`), from the end of Bancroft Way at Warring St to Prospect St; the drivable Bancroft Way west of Warring St (way 6403136) carries `tiger:name_base_1=Bancroft Steps`. The County record most likely took the stairway's name. | **applied**: OSM way cited in the correction; nothing in the stairway was made drivable. |
| K3 | **City layers:** `street_centerline_city` Seg-00193 covers the link as pavement section 627042086, named "CANYON RD" (PAV_BEG_LOC Prospect St, PAV_END_LOC Panoramic Way), **30 ft wide, 2 lanes, two-way** (ONEWAY blank), SPEED 25, PARKING 0 (an undocumented code, G3). `narrow_roads` has no record for it. The City's AB 747 street layer (Link_ID 2565, "BANCROFT STEPS WAY") gives it 30 ft, LANES 1, **capacity 1,125 vehicles/h**, `Narrow_Road` No; the fire-apparatus centerlines (FID 2509, same name) give `No_FireRte` 0. The names disagree (Canyon Rd, Bancroft Steps Way, Bancroft Way); every City layer treats it as a street. | **applied**: width 30 ft and the City match come from Seg-00193 (matched automatically once the link is a street). Lanes stay the hill rule (one each way, which the 2 lanes agree with). | K4 | **Reports:** the SB 99 report (KLD, Rev. 3, p. 4) puts the whole Panoramic Way neighborhood's "only one access point to the nearest evacuation route (at a stop-controlled intersection at Panoramic Way and Prospect St/Canyon Rd)". F-4); it never mentions Rim Rd, Stadium Rim Rd or Canyon Rd. No conflict among OSM, the City layers and the two reports; the County record is the outlier. |
| K5 | **Other County no-vehicle segments.** Of the County file's 109 TRVLWAY NT segments and 66 segments typed or named as paths, steps or walks, 6 have an OSM residential-or-higher way within 10 m on average. Only edge 2322 changes; every other edge is identical apart from node numbering. Effects in the calibration recheck below. |
| K7 | **Found while checking routing, not fixed: most of Panoramic Hill drives into a zone gap.** The Genasys zone BER-013 ("Panoramic Hill Oakland", Genasys WFS) covers the Oakland part of Panoramic Hill east to about 122.238° W: upper Dwight Way, Dwight Place, and Panoramic Way and Panoramic Place in Oakland. The builder takes Berkeley-prefixed zones from the City layer, whose BER-E013 is a sliver along the city line (122.2455–122.2447° W, 83 people, no street midpoint inside it; the X4 problem, which the X4 fix handled only for zones with Oakland, Albany and Emeryville prefixes).
