The R04 calibration match (within 10 min of the study) rests partly on this. | **fixed 2026-11-01** (Z1–Z2): BER-013 now takes its Genasys outline and keeps the City layer's Genasys estimate (83 people). No R04 car leaves the order area into the gap any more; effects in the calibration recheck below and in P26. |
| K8 | **The Crossways rechecked, 2026-11-01: the sources conflict.** For a street: OSM way 6391261 is `highway=residential`, `surface=asphalt`, no access tag, with a stop sign on the way itself (node 10819110534, `highway=stop`, added 2023-04-17, way version 5; queried from overpass-api.de on 2026-11-01), and OSM maps houses numbered 202, 205 and 209 The Crossways; the City's pavement-management fields in `street_centerline_city` (Seg-02488) give a pavement section "THE CROSSWAYS", Overlook Rd to Middlefield Rd, 21 ft, 2 lanes, condition index 62; the City `Narrow_Roads` survey lists it (Link_ID 1503, 21 ft, 7 ft with parking); the AB 747 street layer gives it capacity 1,125 veh/h and a volume-to-capacity ratio of 0.41–0.44, so KLD's model sent traffic over it. Against: the County record (OBJECTID 35701, SEGID 181035806) codes TRVLWAY NT, and the same City segment's network fields code it as a walkway: CATEGORY "PEDESTRIAN", NET_CFCC A72 (the Census code for a stairway or stepped road), SPEED 1, drive times FT_MINUTES and TF_MINUTES −1; its slope is 21%. The OSM way is an unreviewed TIGER import (`tiger:reviewed=no`, `tiger:cfcc=A41`). For the Bancroft link (K3) every City layer agreed it was a street; here two City fields disagree with each other. | **applied 2026-11-01 (Eric chose the street; the conflict is recorded, not settled by a field check)**: second entry in `network.segmentCorrections` (SEGID 181035806, OBJECTID 35701, county {STREET "THE CROSSWAYS", TRVLWAY "NT"}, set {TRVLWAY "B"}, osmWay 6391261), checked against the County record before applying (OBJECTID, SEGID, STREET and TRVLWAY NT as listed; 2 vertices; addresses 200–299; created and revised 2015-09-26). The builder now matches it to the City segment Seg-02488 and the narrow-roads survey: 6.4 m (21 ft) pavement, 2.1 m (7 ft) clear with both sides parked, so it is one of the segments too narrow for an engine in its nominal state; the City's SPEED 1 is not used, because hill streets take the hill speed. Only that edge changes: 6,585 → 6,586 vehicle edges, narrow-roads matches 326 → 327, every other edge identical. Effects: two small dead ends on Overlook Rd and Middlefield Rd shrink (9.0 → 5.4 and 12.8 → 7.3 residents behind a choke, AC16); an engine reaches The Crossways in 1.8–2.1 min at ignition in seeds 1–5; calibration moves stay within seed noise (recheck below). A look at the street, or the City's answer, would still settle the conflict. |


## Zone outlines clipped at the city line, 2026-11-01

Follow-up to K7. Files: `data/raw/city/evac_zones.geojson` (City of Berkeley GenasysZonesBerkeley layer), `data/raw/genasys/zones.json` (Genasys Protect WFS, retrieved 2026-11-01), `data/raw/census/blocks-2020.json`, `data/model/network.json`. Areas sampled on a 5 m lattice; street lengths are vehicle segments whose midpoint lies in the outline. Checked with scratch scripts (not in the repository).

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| Z1 | **The X4 sliver problem also hits Berkeley and Lab zones.** The City layer clips some of them at the city line, and every street beyond the line then lies in no zone. City-layer area → Genasys area, and street length that lay in no zone before the fix: BER-013 "Panoramic Hill Oakland" 0.3 → 21.8 ha, 2,405 m (Dwight Wy, Dwight Pl, Panoramic Wy and Panoramic Pl in Oakland); BER-074 1.9 → 15.3 ha, 1,813 m; BER-075 3.5 → 20.3 ha, 2,327 m; BER-069 12.6 → 21.7 ha, 1,506 m (all three reach south of Alcatraz Av into Oakland); LBL-004 0.8 → 8.5 ha, 765 m (McMillian Rd, Centennial Dr); LBL-005 0.0 → 9.1 ha, 268 m; LBL-006 missing from the City layer, 18.4 ha in Genasys, 43 m; and along the county or Albany line BER-031 29.2 → 31.4 ha (Summit Rd, 345 m), BER-016 37.3 → 37.5 ha (Wildcat Canyon Rd, 344 m), BER-022 (Tulare Av, 227 m), BER-072 29.3 → 30.5 ha (Claremont Av, 212 m). 10.3 km of street in all. The other Berkeley zones match Genasys within 0.1 ha, except where Genasys has split and renumbered a City zone (BER-023, -034, -035, -047, -048, -049, -050 → BER-123, -134, -135, -147, -148, -149, -150; LBL-001 to -003); those City outlines cover the Genasys ones and are kept. | **applied** in `tools/build-network.mjs` (model rule for choosing the outline; outlines sourced): a Berkeley or Lab zone takes its Genasys outline when that outline holds at least 95% of the City outline and adds at least 0.1 ha that no City zone covers; a Berkeley or Lab zone only in Genasys is added if City zones cover under 5% of it. 11 zones qualify (the ones listed). `layers.json` marks each zone's `outlineSrc`; the inspector shows an "Outline" row. Remaining street pieces in no zone near these lines are under 0.1 ha of outline each (Woodmont Av 84 m, Solano Av 74 m, Arlington Av 58 m, Page St 53 m, Grizzly Peak Bl 17 m). |
| Z2 | **Populations for the corrected outlines.** The City layer's Genasys estimate covers the whole zone (its sliver holds no Census population): BER-013 83 people (2020 Census blocks in the Genasys outline: 122); BER-074 1,080 (Census 1,141); BER-075 1,302 (1,218); BER-069 1,235 (1,265); BER-031 618 (458); BER-072 1,199 (1,213); BER-022 1,679 (1,674); BER-016 273 (741, the same gap as before the fix: the outline barely changed); LBL-004 to -006 0. Before the fix BER-074, -075 and -069 put 3,617 people on 3,057 m of Berkeley street (BER-074: 1,080 people on 400 m; access-constraints flagged it as crowded); BER-013's 83 people were not placed at all (AC6). | **applied**: the Genasys estimate stays for every zone that has one (popSrc genasys); LBL-006 has none and takes the Census count (0). **Conflict:** for BER-013 the Census count is 47% higher than Genasys (122 vs 83). **Resolved 2026-11-01 (Eric): BER-013 uses the Census count, 122**, through `population.overrides` as for OKL-003 (X6); `layers.json` gives pop 122, popModeled 122, popSrc census2020, genasysPop 83. Berkeley-prefixed zones now total 121,798 people (was 121,759). Effects: AC16, P29, ER4 and the calibration recheck below. |
| Z3 | Employees, students and visitors are placed on streets by Berkeley zone (Time Study Table 3-5 areas). With Z1, Oakland streets in BER-069, -074, -075 and -013 would have drawn Berkeley's non-residents. | **applied (model rule)**: `js/sim.js` skips OpenStreetMap streets outside the city limits when placing non-residents. Lab streets beyond the line (County segments in LBL-004 to -006) do receive LBNL employees: the Lab is one employer on both sides of the line. |
| Z4 | **Remaining free exits next to order zones (reported, not fixed).** A vehicle counts as out of the order area at any node whose 30 m fire-grid cell lies in no ordered or warned zone (`js/sim.js`). Two kinds remain. (a) Genasys itself leaves gaps along the county line and the freeways: Wildcat Canyon Rd (2.2 km, 15–45 m wide between BER-016/BER-007 and Contra Costa zone CCC-140), short pieces of Grizzly Peak Bl, Golf Course Dr, Shasta Rd and Central Park Dr at the Tilden edge, Arlington Av and Rugby Av at the Kensington line, I-80, CA-24 and the Warren Freeway. Driving onto Wildcat Canyon Rd leads into CCC-140, which no calibration region orders, so the gap moves the exit by meters, not minutes. (b) A grid artifact: nodes whose point lies inside an ordered zone but whose 30 m cell centre falls in a hairline gap between zone outlines also count as out, e.g. Shasta Rd / Keeler Av (inside BER-018), Grizzly Peak Bl / Spruce St / Woodmont Av (BER-006), Wildcat Canyon Rd / Sunset Ln (BER-007), Colusa Av (BER-011), El Camino Real (BER-078), CA-24 (OKL-018). In seed 1 at S4, 3,539 of 25,170 R02 order-zone vehicles and 4,224 of 17,087 R05 vehicles leave the order area at a no-zone node, most at Shasta Rd into Wildcat Canyon Rd (2,136 and 975) and the junction nodes in (b) (Keeler Av 414 and 346, Grizzly Peak Bl 453 and 832). Before Z1 the R02 count was 6,103, including 1,228 into the Panoramic Hill gap and 1,515 into the LBNL gap. | **pending (Eric)**: (b) has a different cause from Z1, so it was measured, not changed. Testing the node point against the zone outlines when its cell is in no zone (a few lines in `js/sim.js`) changed every 90th percentile by 1 min or less in a trial run (R02 and R05 all cases, R04 S1–S4, median of 3 seeds; scratch copy, not committed): the cars drive a few hundred meters farther before counting as out. (a) follows Genasys and is left as is. |
| Z5 | **LBNL jam on weekdays: cause and fix.** With LBL-004 and -005 ordered after Z1, the Lab's internal loop locked on weekdays (S1, S4: all 1,847 LBNL employee vehicles present, Time Study Table 3-5): `tests/stuck.mjs ref1923 S4 100` listed 29 full links, both directions full on Lawrence Rd, McMillian Rd, Segre Rd, Chamberlain Rd, Alvarez Rd and Cyclotron Rd, heads waiting 15–73 min; gridlock relief fired 359 (S1) and 489 (S4) times per R02 run (0 and 30 before Z1). **Cause:** the network had only one way out of the Lab. The County street file stops the Lab's streets at the city line (McMillian Rd at -122.24513, 37.87698; Lee Rd at -122.24597, 37.87522), and the OSM extract (`east-bay-roads.overpassql`) leaves out `highway=service`, which is how OSM tags the Lab's roads beyond the line: McMillan Road (way 38895987) to the **Grizzly Peak Gate** and Lawrence Road (way 8013488, bus=yes) to the **Strawberry Canyon Gate**, both on Centennial Dr (OSM gate nodes 462036035, 5843474846, 7782302882, 7782319497; Overpass, 2026-11-01). Before Z1 the missing roads did not matter, because Lab cars counted as out once they reached McMillian Rd in the zone gap. After Z1 all 1,851 Lab vehicles (seed 1) had to leave by Cyclotron Rd to Hearst Av (the Blackberry Canyon Entrance), one lane at about 900 veh/h: the last left at t+4 h, and cars re-routing between the loop's two directions filled every two-way link both ways. Lab parking was not the cause: with three exits the same placement clears. OSM also tags parts of Chamberlain Rd, Alvarez Rd, Glaser Rd and Lawrence Rd one-way, where the County file codes them two-way (**conflict noted, not resolved**: the County coding stays; it did not cause the lock-up). | **fixed 2026-11-01**: `tools/build-network.mjs` adds labeled OSM stretches (`network.osmAdditions`: McMillan Road from the County end to the Grizzly Peak Gate pieces, about 95 m; Lawrence Road from the County end of Lee Rd to the Strawberry Canyon Gate, about 700 m; the gate's inbound and outbound one-way pieces; raw data `data/raw/osm/lbnl-gate-roads.json`, sourced). Model rule `network.labGatesExitOnly`: Lab streets (County streets in LBL zones and OSM ways there tagged access=private) lose the direction that enters them from a public street, and the Grizzly Peak Gate's inbound approach loop closes with it, so cars leave by any of the three gates and none drive through the Lab (with the gates open both ways, 24 Northside cars from BER-040, -042 and -043 cut through it in R02 S4, seed 1); engines pass. R02 S4, seed 1: Lab vehicles leave by the Grizzly Peak Gate 773, Strawberry Canyon 687, Blackberry Canyon 391; the last leaves between t+1 h 25 min and t+1 h 30 min (was t+4 h); no Lab link appears in `tests/stuck.mjs` at t+45, 60, 75 or 100 min; gridlock relief 489 → 48, all of it on UC campus roads (BER-042, -043), Southside streets and the Marin circle. How the Lab actually runs its gates in an evacuation is an information gap. Calibration recheck below. Correction after Codex review on PR #14: gate pieces left with no direction were first marked non-vehicle, which also shut out engines; they are now vehicle streets closed by a gate (residents' cars excluded, engines pass after the barrier delay, no parking in the gate lane). Calibration, engine-blocked counts and the access analysis are unchanged; engine routes through the gates are restored. |

## Simple-view presets: robustness test, 2026-11-01

1,140 runs: scenarios ref1923, eastWind, panoramic and eastWindDay at low, central and high severity, seeds 1–5, each with 19 plan variants (fire on). A preset qualifies only if Plan B beats Plan A on its headline measure in every same-seed pair. Medians below are central severity. All are model results on synthetic households, not predictions.

| # | Finding | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| P10 | Half of Fire Zones 2–3 households leaving on a Red Flag warning cuts people still there when embers arrived in all 60 pairs: eastWind about 5,200 → 3,500, ref1923 about 6,000 → 3,700 (−26% to −43% by seed; −16% to −19% at high severity). 90% clearance improves 1–15 min. Limited to the City's Leave Early Zones, the direction holds in 60 of 60 but the effect is only 1–12% at central and high severity. | **applied**: preset "Leave early on a Red Flag day" uses Fire Zones 2–3. |
| P11 | First order at 5 min instead of 20 cuts people still there when embers arrived in all 60 pairs: ref1923 about 6,900 → 5,500 (−20% every seed), eastWind about 5,700 → 4,900. 90% clearance changes by a median of −0.3 min. | **applied**: preset "Decide faster" (ref1923, S4). |
| P12 | Same fire and plan, fall weekday (S4) vs night (S6), eastWind: 90% clearance about 107 → 93 min, 13–19 min shorter in all 15 runs; about 17,800 vs 15,700 evacuating vehicles. | **applied**: preset "Weekday midday or night". In ref1923 the time gain is only 3–7 min at low and central severity. |
| P13 | (Superseded as the parking question's headline by ER2, 2026-11-01.) Hill parking options leave evacuation time unchanged (median change 0.0–0.2 min in every scenario) but cut segments too narrow for an engine in every run: eastWind S6 about 194 → 107 (one side), 75 (none); tight-to-curb and narrow-roads-only are milder (113–137). Engine travel time at ignition is 0.3–4.6 min and never blocked, so it is not a useful headline. | **applied**: preset "Parked cars and fire engines" headlines blocked segments. |
| P14 | Ordering all hill zones at ignition + 10 or + 30 min has no consistent effect (median about 0%; better in only 4–11 of 15 pairs per scenario; + 30 min slows 90% clearance by about 11 min in ref1923). | **pending**: check whether the setting works as intended; kept out of simple view. |
| P15 | Ordering every zone within 1–2 km of the fire orders about 110,000 people instead of 27,000–31,000, roughly doubles peak queues and pushes 90% clearance from about 95 to 150–240 min. It helps in eastWind (14 of 15) but is worse in Panoramic Hill in all 15 pairs (+10% to +36% still there when embers arrived) and in ref1923 at high severity. | **context**: a possible "wider orders can backfire" lesson for expert view; rests on model rules for junction capacity. |
| P16 | In Panoramic Hill most people still there when embers arrived are in zones with no order (9,444 of about 15,700, central, seed 1), and in 10 of 15 runs not all ordered vehicles clear within 240 min. | **context**: Panoramic preset dropped from simple view. |
| P17 | At high severity most of the count is people in zones that never got an order (eastWind seed 1: 21,768 of 37,000), so plan effects shrink to a few percent. Seed-to-seed spread (eastWind central 2,935–9,298) is larger than most plan effects, which is why every comparison pairs runs on the same seed. | **context**: simple view defaults to central severity and compares same-seed pairs. |
| P18 | Recheck 2026-11-01 on the final network (streets beyond Berkeley, City diverters, OKL-003 from the Census; 120 runs, 3 severities × 5 seeds): every preset still wins in 15 of 15 same-seed pairs. Central medians: leave early (Fire Zones 2–3) 5,080 → 3,647 still there when embers arrived, 90% clearance 92 → 91 min; Leave Early Zones only 5,080 → 4,858; decide faster 6,807 → 5,460, clearance unchanged; weekday vs night 105 vs 92 min, 17,214 vs 15,063 vehicles; one-side parking 284 → 143 segments too narrow for an engine, clearance unchanged. | **applied**: preset texts updated with these numbers. |
| P19 | Per-fire test for a Simple-view fire choice, 2026-11-01: 360 runs, fires eastWind, ref1923, panoramic and a trial Claremont Canyon fire (P22) at low, central and high severity, seeds 1–5, same seed for A and B. Each question keeps its time of day on every fire: leave early and parking at S6 (fall night), decide faster at S4 (fall weekday midday, as on the 1923 fire it was built on), weekday vs night S4 against S6. eastWindDay was not tested separately: it is the eastWind fire at S4, which the questions already set. Rule: a question is offered on a fire if Plan B beats Plan A on the headline measure in 15 of 15 pairs and the central-severity median changes by 5% or more; a missing 90% time counts as a failure. Results in the table below. | **applied**: Simple view has a Fire row; each question is switched off, with a one-line reason, on a fire where it fails. "What to look for" gives each fire's central medians. |
| P20 | Weekday vs night on the 1923 fire passes the win count (15 of 15) but the central gap is 4 min (95 vs 91 min, −5%). For region R02 the 2025 Evacuation Time Study gives 175 min on a fall weekday and 105 at night (Table 7-1), a 70-min gap; the simulator runs 50–80 min fast on weekdays there (calibration check). Offering it would teach a gap about a seventeenth of the study's. | **applied**: withheld on the 1923 fire, with that reason. A judgment beyond the P19 rule. |
| P21 | Panoramic Hill: in 10 of 15 runs per time of day (every central and high run) not all ordered cars leave the order area within the 240-min run, so there is no 90% time and weekday vs night fails. Leave early (−21% central) and decide faster (−9% central, −3% to −19% by run) pass, but most people still there when embers arrived live in zones this fire never orders (about 9,000 of 15,000, central). | **applied**: weekday vs night withheld; the other two questions say where the people still there live. |
| P22 | Trial fourth fire, Claremont Canyon: ignition at −122.2250, 37.8640 (Claremont Canyon Regional Preserve, north side), wind from 60° at 32 mph, 240 min, default S6, City region R10 "South Berkeley Hills" ordered (11 zones, about 8,400 people: Panoramic Hill, Claremont, the Southside east of College Ave, Upper Rockridge, Oakland's Claremont Hills). All four questions pass, but none teaches anything new: weekday vs night matches the east-wind fire (106 vs 92 min), and most people still there are in zones the fire never orders (about 5,300 of 6,800, central), as on Panoramic Hill. | **not applied**: left out of `js/scenarios.js`; the definition above is enough to restore it, for example to give south-side residents a fire of their own. |
| P23 | Panoramic Hill per-fire test rerun after opening the Bancroft Wy link (K6), 2026-11-01, same 90 runs as P19 (low, central, high × seeds 1–5, same seed for A and B). Leave early: central 14,879 → 11,925 (−20%; −7% to −43% by run), 15 of 15. Decide faster: 16,787 → 15,294 (−9%; −3% to −19%), 15 of 15. Weekday vs night: still no 90% time in 10 of 15 runs per time of day (every central and high run); −10% to −17% in the 5 low runs. One-side parking: 284 → 143, 15 of 15 (unchanged). Ordered people 2,081 (was 2,086); people still there in zones this fire never orders about 9,180 central. Before the change the same runs reproduced the P19 table exactly. | **applied**: offered and withheld questions unchanged; "What to look for" numbers updated to about 14,900 against 11,900 and 16,800 against 15,300. |
| P24 | Model bug found while answering the Codex review of PR #2 (Future growth settings and R0), 2026-11-01: in `buildHouseholds` (`js/sim.js`) the leave-early draw came from the shared random stream only when `earlyShare` was above 0, interleaved with household placement. A leave-early plan therefore placed different households (eastWind seed 1: 83,001 against 83,017) and shifted every later vehicle count, mobilization time and ride, so "Leave early" was not compared like for like. An audit of the constructor found more plan-dependent draws: households leaving early skipped their mobilization and ride draws, and zones a plan orders that the scenario does not (`alertKm`, `hillsOrderMin`) skipped the warning-compliance and shadow draws, for residents and non-resident cars. `orderDelayMin` and `hillsParking` changed no draws; parking (`js/parking.js`) and the fire already used fixed draws from the seed. On main, `tests/plan-draws.mjs` fails 27 of 81 comparisons. | **applied**: the leave-early decision has a separate stream, one draw per household. Every other shared-stream draw follows the scenario's orders alone; a number a plan needs that the scenario did not draw comes from a per-household (or per-car) stream with a fixed slot per decision. A carless household whose Plan A neighbor left early rides with another neighbor. Baseline runs (DEFAULT_PLAN) are unchanged: `tests/headless.mjs` baseline lines and `tests/calibration.mjs` output match main exactly; only the headless "Early+narrow" plan moves. `tests/plan-draws.mjs` now passes 81 of 81. Leave-early recheck in P25. Merged with future growth and R0 (R6), 2026-11-01: an added unit takes its draws in the same scenario pattern from its own stream, with supplementary slots keyed by its index among all units, so R0 decides which units are built and does not change the draws of the rest; a ride for a household of today's that the shared stream does not draw uses R6's per-household stream. A carless household of today's that rides with an added unit has no ride where R0 bars every added unit on its street. With DEFAULT_PLAN, `tests/headless.mjs` baseline lines, `tests/calibration.mjs` and `tests/growth.mjs` (eastWind, ref1923, panoramic × central, high × seeds 1–5 × 7 settings, 210 runs) print exactly what main prints, so R3–R5 do not move. `tests/plan-draws.mjs` passes 180 of 180, 99 of them with maximum growth: R0 none against each R0 area, the leave-early plans with and without R0, and growth none against maximum growth on today's households. |
| P25 | Leave-early question rerun after P24, 2026-11-01, on the network with the Bancroft Wy link open (K6, P23): 135 runs, fires eastWind, ref1923 and panoramic, low, central and high severity, seeds 1–5. The parking count covers hill streets citywide and depends on the seed and time of day, not on the fire, so it is identical on every fire. Hill parking leaves the 90% time unchanged (median change 0 min on every fire with a time). All of these are model results on synthetic households and illustrative fires, not predictions.

## Engine reach within the City's 5-minute standard, 2026-11-01

Asked for by Eric (2026-11-01, row above in his section): a measure that shows what parking rules do for engine access, since engine travel time to the fire edge is the same under every parking plan. All numbers are model results on synthetic households, not predictions.

| # | Finding | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| ER1 | Rule (model rule, `engineTimes`, `engineReachTimes` and `engineReachStats` in `js/sim.js`): shortest engine drive from any of the 7 Berkeley stations in the `fireStations` layer, run once at ignition with no fire and no traffic, on the same rules as the engine route to the fire edge: streets driven both ways, a segment passes only where parked cars leave at least `access.engineClearM` (10 ft) under the plan's parking rule and the seed's parked cars, fixed barriers block, gates and removable bollards cost `access.barrierDelayS` (3 min), speed 1.15 × posted speed. A home is reached at the nearer end of its street segment; its own segment need not pass. Counted for households in Berkeley's evacuation zones (`BER-`), because the station layer holds no Oakland, Kensington or Albany stations; "hill" is Fire Zones 2–3 on the household's segment (for the few `BER-` streets inside Oakland, the 90 m elevation proxy). Outputs in `summary()`: `engineReach5Share`, `engineReach5People`, `engineReachUnreachablePeople` (no engine route), `engineReachBeyond5People`, the `…All` versions for every `BER-` household, and `engineReach5ShareNow`, the same share every 5 min with traffic and fire (a segment the fire has reached counts as not reached). No random draws: `tests/plan-draws.mjs` passes 180 of 180. | **applied**: results row "Hill homes an engine can reach within 5 minutes (City standard)" with share and people and a tooltip on the rule; two Compare rows (cannot reach within 5 min; no engine route); street color mode "Engine drive time (plan and seed)": within 5 min green, 5–8 amber, over 8 red, no route dark red; intro, Assumptions & limits and README text. |
| ER2 | Robustness, 225 runs: fires eastWind, ref1923 and panoramic, low, central and high severity, seeds 1–5, night (S6), each parking option against status quo. Reach at ignition depends on the seed's parked cars only, so it is identical on every fire and severity; the 15 pairs per fire are 5 distinct seeds. Hill residents in `BER-` zones: 27,798–27,881. Central medians, A (status quo) → B, cannot reach within 5 min: **one side** 1,510 → 1,010 (−33%; −11% to −93% by run), share reached 94.6% → 96.4%, no route 1,377 → 1,010; **tight to the curb** 1,510 → 1,232 (−18%; −7% to −83%); **no parking on narrow roads** 1,510 → 1,211 (−20%; −10% to −64%); **none in Fire Zones 2–3** 1,510 → 0 (share 100% in every run). B better in 15 of 15 pairs on all three fires for every option. All households in `BER-` zones, no route: one side 1,723 → 1,327, none 1,723 → 315. End of run, with traffic and fire, share reached, one side: eastWind 83.3% → 85.5%, ref1923 67.1% → 70.7%, panoramic 80.3% → 82.8%, B better in 15 of 15 on each. Unchanged: people still there when embers arrived (B better in 5–9 of 15 per fire and option, changes of −4% to +8%), 90% clearance (median change 0.0–0.2 min), engine travel to the fire edge (identical). On the share itself the central change is +1.9% for one side, under P19's 5% bar; on residents not reached it is −33%, over it. | **applied**: the parking question's headline is now "Hill residents an engine cannot reach within 5 minutes" (P19 rule met on all three fires); "What to look for" rewritten with these numbers and per-fire end-of-run shares; segments too narrow for an engine (283 → 143) kept as a second line. Chosen over the segment count because it counts people and holds in every pair. |
| ER3 | Where the measure comes from. At posted speed × 1.15 nearly every hill home with an engine route is within 5 minutes (median 133 people beyond 5 min but reachable under status quo, none under one side), so the measure mostly counts homes that parked cars cut off. Under one-side parking about 950 of the remaining 1,010 are Panoramic Hill (BER-058 about 870, BER-013 about 85) in 4 of 5 seeds: the lowest block of Panoramic Way (49 m, City pavement width 5.2 m, about 17 ft) is the only block of the street wide enough for residents to park (the 4.6 m blocks leave under 8 ft, so no one parks there), and a car there leaves 2.8 m or less, under the 3.05 m an engine needs. In seed 4 no car is drawn on that block. Real engine speeds on hill streets (turns, grades, narrow passing) would push some homes past 5 minutes; A6 says parcels outside the 5-minute area exist, which this model's speeds do not reproduce. | **context**: stated in Assumptions & limits and README. For Eric: whether residents park on that block of Panoramic Way, and how wide it is between the curbs, decides the largest part of this result (a field check, like the tape measure at Poppy Ln in HANDOFF). |
| ER4 | Rerun 2026-11-01 after opening The Crossways (K8) and taking BER-013's Census count (Z2): the ER2 runs for status quo against one side (90 runs: three fires, low, central and high, seeds 1–5, S6). Hill residents in `BER-` zones 27,855–27,914 (27,798–27,881 before). Central medians, cannot reach within 5 min: 1,541 → 1,050 (−32%; −11% to −92% by run; ER2 1,510 → 1,010); share reached 94.5% → 96.2%; no route 1,408 → 1,050; reachable but beyond 5 min 133 → 0. B better in 15 of 15 on all three fires. End-of-run share, one side: eastWind 83.2% → 85.4%, ref1923 67.1% → 70.7%, panoramic 80.2% → 82.7%, 15 of 15 each. Segments too narrow for an engine 283 → 143 (unchanged: The Crossways is too narrow in its nominal state, but the seeds' parked cars leave it 2.9–4.0 m). By zone under one side (scratch script, eastWind central, S6): in seeds 1, 2, 3 and 5 Panoramic Hill accounts for 985–993 of the 1,038–1,088 people not reached (BER-058 865–877, BER-013 119–123; ER3 had about 85 for BER-013); in seed 4 none, 78 people in all. | **applied**: `js/presets.js` parking text now says about 1,550 of 27,900 with today's parking, about 1,050 with one side, and about 990 cut off on Panoramic Hill. |

## EMBER (Effective Mitigations for Berkeley's Ember Resilience), City of Berkeley, 2025

Sources: Special Item 01a, "Effective Mitigations for Berkeley's Ember Resilience – Fire Code", Council special meeting Feb 11, 2025 (53 pp.); Item 43c, "EMBER Implementation Plan + Vegetation Management Committee + Clarifying Code", June 17, 2025 (22 pp.); Special Item 1d, Safe Passages (Feb 11, 2025). PDFs at berkeleyca.gov; read 2026-11-01.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | EMBER's levels of strictness are by distance from the house and by area. Distance: Zone 0, no combustible material within 5 ft of a structure, plus Home Ignition Zones 1 and 2 farther out (01a p. 2). Area, phased: I, Berkeley Fire Zones 3 and 4; II, other high-risk parts of Fire Zone 2; III, the rest of CAL FIRE's Very High Fire Hazard Severity Zone (01a p. 6). The Fire Department calls moving "over 9,000 parcels" through this "too long" and asks to concentrate on one area at a time, because a property's resilience "only increases if done in conjunction with adjacent properties" (01a p. 6). Council adopted Zone 0 for the Grizzly Peak and Panoramic Hill Mitigation Areas, effective Jan 1, 2026 (43c p. 2). | **pending**: the natural settings for a vegetation intervention are area (Phase I, II, III) and compliance share. |
| M2 | The evidence EMBER cites is about structure survival, not evacuation, and gives no dose-response curve. Syphard et al. (over 2,000 structures, San Diego County): reducing woody vegetation cover "up to 40%" next to structures and keeping vegetation off them were the most effective actions. Gibbons et al. 2012 (2009 Black Saturday fires): tree and shrub cover within 40 m was the top predictor of house loss, and treatments were more effective the closer to the house. Milliman 2023 for Paradise: combined home hardening, zoning and buffers could cut losses "by up to 75%" and premiums by up to 55% (43c pp. 11–12). | **pending**: an upper bound for a structure-loss setting once buildings are modeled (Phase 3). No source gives an effect on fire spread rate or evacuation time. |
| M3 | The Fire Department argues against relying on wildland thinning: "limited evidence around vegetation thinning beyond the immediate area around homes suggests limited effectiveness", one study finds thinning may increase crown fire, and "In all research, extreme weather appears to nullify most interventions" (01a pp. 6–7). | **context**: a reason to model vegetation as near-home (structure ignition, house-to-house spread) rather than as slower wildland spread, and to show its effect shrinking at high severity. |
| M4 | House-to-house spread is the main danger once fire reaches the hills: homes are close together, and "Fighting just one house fire typically requires five to six fire apparatus and at least 15 firefighters" (01a p. 7). The aim of Zone 0 is to slow the "transition from vegetative fire to structural conflagration" to give firefighters and evacuees time. | **pending**: links vegetation to evacuation only through time; used in the staffing design note (`docs/design/fire-staffing.md`, FS11). |
| M5 | Roadside: the City's Grizzly Peak Project removes privacy hedges within 10 ft of the road on private property and offers free replacement plants (43c p. 15). | **pending**: a possible "roadside clearing" setting on evacuation routes; no source quantifies its effect on drivers' exposure. |

## California ADU law and Berkeley's hill ADU rules (public sources, read 2026-11-01)

Researched for the R0 setting (Eric, 2026-11-01). Statute text was read on california.public.law's mirror of leginfo (leginfo.legislature.ca.gov refused automated requests); the mirror shows each section current to 2026. Inferences are marked as ours.

| # | Insight (source) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | **Area designation.** A local ADU ordinance may "Designate areas within the jurisdiction of the local agency where accessory dwelling units may be permitted. The designation of areas may be based on the adequacy of water and sewer services and the impact of accessory dwelling units on traffic flow and public safety." Gov. Code §66314(a) (https://california.public.law/codes/government_code_section_66314), same wording as former §65852.2(a)(1)(A); recodified by SB 477 (2024). §66315 makes §66314 the maximum standards; §66316 requires ministerial approval. | **applied**: R0 legal note, "what current law allows". |
| L2 | **By-right units.** "Notwithstanding Sections 66314 to 66322," a city "shall ministerially approve" on a single-family lot one ADU and one JADU within existing or proposed space, and one detached new-construction ADU of up to 800 sq ft with 4 ft setbacks (Gov. Code §66323(a)); §66323(b) bars other standards. The only fire term is that setbacks be "sufficient for fire and safety" ((a)(1)(C)); sprinklers only if the main house needs them (§66323(d)). No fire or evacuation exception. | **applied**: R0 legal note. Our reading: the Time Study's 2 ADUs + 1 JADU per parcel (E26) is the §66323 combination, so all 27,060 units of its maximum are by-right units. |
| L3 | **HCD guidance.** ADU Handbook, March 2026 edition, pp. 42–43 (https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/policy-and-research/adu-handbook-update.pdf): area limits "should be accompanied by detailed findings of fact"; Fire Hazard Severity Zone maps "are not intended to restrict housing development" and are "not a conclusive rationale"; designated areas "cannot preclude units described in Government Code section 66323"; limiting those needs a §66300(b)(1)(B) moratorium, "only with HCD's approval", against an "imminent threat to … health and safety". | **applied**: R0 legal note. |
| L4 | **HCD on Berkeley's 2022 hill limit.** Ord. 7,799-N.S. (BMC 12.99, Feb. 8, 2022) allowed one ADU or JADU per Hillside Overlay lot. HCD (letter of Oct. 17, 2022, pp. 2–3, https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/policy-and-research/ordinance-review-letters/Berkeley-ADU-101722.pdf) found Berkeley "has not adequately demonstrated that new ADUs will actually impact public safety in the VHFHSZ": the Wong (2021) evacuation simulation "is not specific to ADUs and JADUs" and gives no data on "how many lots are likely to add ADUs"; and "even if the City would provide adequate justification" the limit could not reach by-right units. Council repealed BMC 12.99 on Oct. 3, 2023 (Item 10), effective Nov. 9, 2023. HCD's letter of June 16, 2026 (finding 1, https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/policy-and-research/ordinance-review-letters/berkeley-adu-findings-061626.pdf): Berkeley "must … allow the full allotment of units described under section 66323 and at least one unit described under section 66314." | **context**: the evidence an area restriction needs (ADU-specific, how many lots, effect per unit). The Time Study's ADU sensitivity (E26) is closer to that than the Wong study; HCD has not ruled on it (our inference). |
| L5 | **SB 9** (two units per lot, lot splits; Gov. Code §65852.21, §66411.7). Site exclusions incorporate §65913.4(a)(6)(B)–(K) as of Sept. 16, 2021, which excludes very high fire hazard severity zones (VHFHSZ) but not sites with fire hazard mitigation measures (Building Code ch. 7A, PRC §4290–4291); the 2021 wording that controls was not retrieved. In practice hill sites qualify: Executive Order N-32-25 (July 30, 2025) says "SB 9 already requires development in these zones to meet updated ingress and egress standards"; Berkeley's SB 9 eligibility sheet says VHFHSZ sites "can proceed but may have special building requirements". Denial requires a building official's written finding, "based upon a preponderance of the evidence," of a "specific, adverse impact" with no feasible mitigation (§65852.21(d), §66411.7(d)): project by project. Berkeley permitted 2 SB 9 duplexes and 2 lot splits in 2024, none in 2025 (APR). | **applied**: R0 legal note ("deny single SB 9 projects case by case"; area-wide exclusion needs a state change). The EO suspended SB 9 only in the Palisades and Eaton burn areas and was sued (YIMBY Law, LASC 25STCP04761). |
| L6 | **Housing Crisis Act** (Gov. Code §66300): bars "reducing the intensity of land use" below Jan. 1, 2018 zoning ((b)(1)(A)) and caps on units or population ((b)(1)(D)); allows a moratorium only for an imminent threat and with HCD approval ((b)(1)(B)); allows downzoning with "no net loss in residential capacity" elsewhere ((h)(1)). (e)(4): the section "shall not apply to a housing development project located within a very high fire hazard severity zone"; whether that covers a zoning ordinance, as opposed to a single project, is unresolved (no HCD or court reading found). | **applied**: R0 legal note (moratorium route). **Open question**: the (e)(4) reading. |
| L7 | **Bills, 2023–2026.** Chaptered: AB 1033 (2023), SB 477 (2024), SB 1211 (2024), AB 2533 (2024), and SB 543, AB 1154, SB 9 (2025) and AB 462 (2025, effective on signing). None adds a local power to restrict ADUs for fire or evacuation; AB 462 eases ADU occupancy after disasters (§66328(b)). No 2025–26 bill restricting ADUs or SB 9 in a VHFHSZ was found. §65852.21 and §66411.7 show an amendment dated June 30, 2026; the bill was not identified. | **context**. |
| L8 | **Berkeley's current hill rules**: Ord. 7,983-N.S. (passed to print July 29, 2025; Sept. 9, 2025 agenda, Item 5, https://berkeleyca.gov/sites/default/files/documents/2025-09-09%20Item%2005%20Ordinance%207983.pdf). Its findings cite the Time Study: maximum ADU build-out in the Hillside Overlay "could increase evacuation times by as much as 102%". In the Hillside Overlay it caps size (850 sq ft studio or 1 bedroom, 1,000 sq ft 2+ bedrooms, vs 1,200 sq ft elsewhere), detached height (20 ft), and allows detached ADUs only on multifamily lots. It does not limit the count: single-family lots keep "1 Conversion ADU, 1 New Construction ADU, and 1 Junior ADU"; ADU parking requirements are removed citywide. Earlier: ADUs barred in Fire Zone 3 and an Administrative Use Permit in Fire Zone 2 before 2020; a Dec. 10, 2019 urgency ordinance (Item 30) barred ADUs on Fire Zone 2 streets under 26 ft. The Fire Chief's Sept. 13, 2023 letter asked for at least 8 ft between house and ADU in Fire Zones 2–3. No Council referral proposing an ADU ban after the Time Study was found. | **context**: R0 goes beyond current Berkeley rules. Our reading of "detached ADUs only on multifamily lots" against §66323(a)(2) was not checked. |
| L9 | **Berkeley ADU permits** (Housing Element Annual Progress Reports to HCD): permitted 123 (2021), 95 (2023), 102 (2024), 117 (2025); completed 90, 72, 91, 91, 92 (2021–2025; 2022 from the Jan. 20, 2026 Item 19 referral). No count for the hills alone was found; the APR parcel lists could be tallied by zone without keeping addresses. | **applied**: moderate growth (2,706 units, 10% of the maximum) is about 25 years of the citywide pace if every ADU were in the hills (assumption, `growth.options.moderate`). |

**Bottom line for R0.** *What current law allows:* bar ADUs beyond the by-right minimum (more or larger §66314 units) in designated areas such as Fire Zones 2–3, the Leave Early Zones or access-constrained streets, with ADU-specific findings (L1, L3, L4); size, height and form limits like Ord. 7,983 (L8); case-by-case SB 9 denials on a specific adverse impact (L5); a moratorium covering by-right units with HCD's approval on an imminent-threat finding (L3, L6); and, our inference, base-zoning reductions offset by upzoning elsewhere (L6). *What would need a change in state law:* a standing area ban on §66323 by-right units (up to three per single-family lot, which is the whole of the Time Study's maximum); an area-wide SB 9 exclusion in Berkeley's VHFHSZ; a unit or population cap, unless §66300(e)(4) is read to cover ordinances. Additions that add floor area but no unit fall outside ADU and SB 9 law and add no household in the simulator; limiting them by floor area or lot coverage would count as reducing the intensity of land use under §66300(b)(1)(A), subject to the (e)(4) question.

## Future growth and R0: simulator results, 2026-11-01

Model results on synthetic households and an illustrative fire, not a prediction. The settings are in the simulator (Expert view: Future growth, R0). R3 is `GROWTH=max node tests/calibration.mjs` (and `GROWTH=middle`); R4 and R5 are `node tests/growth.mjs <scenario> <severity>` for eastWind, ref1923 and panoramic at central and high severity, with `data/model/access-constraints.json` from the access-constraints branch (commit 51bf7c7) in place. R3–R5 were rerun 2026-11-01 after the two fixes from Codex's review of PR #2 (R6).

| # | Finding | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Where growth goes.** Berkeley's Fire Zones 2–3 hold 899 residential street segments (102 km) and about 11,700 households today (27,750 people) by the simulator's allocation. The maximum build-out (27,060 units) lands on 890 of them, about 2.3 added units per existing household; by Genasys zone the largest shares are BER-040 (3,317 units), BER-057 (2,212) and BER-028 (2,146). The Leave Early Zones hold 7,735 of the 27,060 (29%); streets behind a choke point (`access-constraints.json` as built 2026-11-01 by the parallel access-constraints work) hold 3,054 (11%); Fire Zones 2–3 hold all of them. | **applied**: R0 notes quote these shares. Rerun after the zone-gap merge (R7): access-constrained streets hold 2,998 units (11%). |
| R2 | **Check that growth leaves today's households alone.** With growth none, `node tests/headless.mjs` and `node tests/calibration.mjs` print exactly what they printed before the change (diffed). With maximum growth, R0 in Fire Zones 2–3 reproduces the no-growth run to the person in all 30 robustness runs. | **applied**: HANDOFF gotcha. |
| R3 | **Against the Time Study (fire off, study zones, median of 3 seeds, maximum ADU build-out vs none).** 90th-percentile ETE, fall weekday S4: R02 1:35 → 2:04 (+30%; study +58%, Future Case 3:20 → 5:15, Table 10-12); R04 1:44 → 1:46 (+2%; study +25%, 2:00 → 2:30); R05 1:45 → 1:48 (+3%; study +97%, 2:45 → 5:25). Summer night S3: R02 +8% (study +123%), R04 +2% (study +52%), R05 +2% (study +177%). The study's six-scenario averages are +102%, +52% and +154%. 100th percentile: unchanged in S4 (3:26–3:29), +15 min in S3; the study reports +121% for R02 and R05 and no change for R04. Order-zone vehicles rise R02 25,182 → 44,999, R04 1,204 → 2,865, R05 17,172 → 47,023. Middle housing (870 units) changes no 90th percentile by more than 1 min (study: up to +8%, 0–10 min). | **conflict, disclosed**: the simulator absorbs the added traffic far more easily than the study's network. Its junctions pass traffic faster than the study's signals and stop signs (the same gap that makes it 15–80 min fast today; calibration notes), and the study measures from a 2031 Future Case that already adds 4,238 hill units and 18,205 citywide. Read growth effects on evacuation time as a lower bound. README limits and the Assumptions & limits panel say so. Figures above are from the network before the zone-outline fix; R7 has them on the current network, where Panoramic Hill now rises more than the study. |
| R4 | **Robustness, fire on (default plan, scenario time of day, seeds 1–5, central and high severity).** Maximum growth vs none raises people still there when embers arrived in all 30 same-seed pairs: eastWind central 5,080 → 14,179, high 34,944 → 68,587; ref1923 central 5,850 → 24,935, high 44,310 → 76,072; panoramic central 15,900 → 30,661, high 52,174 → 67,895 (medians). 90% clearance rises in every pair where it is reached: eastWind +12 min (central) and +33 min (high), ref1923 +40 min (central) (median of pairs). In ref1923 at high severity 90% is not reached within the 6 h run in 3 of 5 seeds with maximum growth (110–123 min without), and vehicles cut off by fire rise from 664–1,172 to 2,515–3,235. Panoramic Hill does not reach 90% within 240 min with or without growth (as in P16). Moderate growth (2,706 units, 6,413 people) adds 461–1,822 people still there when embers arrived at central severity (every pair) and 0–3 min to 90% clearance. | **context**: growth matters more for exposure than for clearance time in the simulator. Rerun on the current network in R7; every direction holds. |
| R5 | **R0 by area, maximum growth (same runs).** Every R0 area lowers people still there when embers arrived in all 30 pairs. Central, difference of medians: R0 in the Leave Early Zones (bars 18,332 people) −1,863 eastWind, −4,158 ref1923, −11,192 panoramic; R0 in access-constrained areas (bars 7,238 people) −1,456, −2,456, −5,403; R0 in Fire Zones 2–3 returns the no-growth result in every pair. Per person barred, access-constrained R0 removes more exposure than Leave Early Zones R0 in all three scenarios (0.20 vs 0.10 eastWind, 0.34 vs 0.23 ref1923, 0.75 vs 0.61 panoramic). 90% clearance, in pairs where both runs reach it: Leave Early Zones −2 to −12 min and access −2 to −8 min, lower in every pair in eastWind (both severities) and ref1923 central; at ref1923 high the 6 h cutoff leaves too few comparable pairs. With moderate growth, Leave Early Zones R0 lowers the count in every pair (−93 to −1,271). | **context**: direction consistent in every pair; size depends on scenario. Rerun on the current network in R7; every direction holds. |
| R6 | **Two fixes from Codex's review of PR #2 (2026-11-01).** (a) Switching from Simple back to Expert with growth selected rebuilt the run before the view changed, so the map showed a run without growth while Expert showed the growth setting; the rebuild now happens after the switch. Checked in headless Chrome: maximum growth in Expert, then Simple, then Expert shows the "Future growth" results row again (27,060 · 64,132); before the fix it was missing. (b) A carless household of today's drew its rideshare from the shared random stream only when its street had a donor, so an added household on a street with no donor today added draws and shifted every later rideshare draw and the non-resident vehicles. Codex's repro (1923, central, seed 1, maximum growth, R0 none vs Leave Early Zones) moved employee vehicles 8,844 → 8,843 and on-campus students 1,934 → 1,935; in the same run, maximum growth changed the ride of 1,036 of today's carless households compared with no growth. Now a household of today's with a donor of today's on its street draws as without growth and picks among today's donors; where only added households can give a ride, it draws from a stream of its own. After the fix, in the same run, non-resident vehicles are 8,843 employees, 7,640 off-campus and 1,935 on-campus students and 4,567 visitors under every growth and R0 setting, and the only change to today's households is the 4 carless households whose street has no donor today, who ride with an added household when one is there. With growth none, `node tests/headless.mjs` and `node tests/calibration.mjs` print exactly what main prints (diffed). R4 and R5 medians moved by about 400 people or less; no direction changed. R3's evacuation times are unchanged; R02 order-zone vehicles with maximum growth moved 45,004 → 44,999. The 1923 high-severity range without growth in R4 now reads 110–123 min (the earlier note said 112–123). | **applied**: `js/main.js` setMode, `js/sim.js` buildHouseholds. |
| R7 | **R1, R3–R5 rerun after merging the zone-outline and Lab-gate fixes (Z1–Z5) with main, 2026-11-01.** Growth runs use the default plan, so main's draw fix (P24) does not touch them (P24: `tests/growth.mjs` prints exactly what it printed before); every change below comes from the network and zones. Three changes matter: Panoramic Hill's Oakland part (BER-013) and the Oakland streets of BER-069, -074 and -075 are now in zones, so a car no longer counts as out by driving into the no-zone part of the hill (K7, Z1); the Lab's two Centennial Dr gates are back, exit-only (Z5); and the access-constrained areas were regenerated on this network (AC14, AC15). **R1:** growth lands on the same streets (BER-040 3,316 units, BER-057 2,212, BER-028 2,146; none on Oakland streets, since `inBerkeleyHills` excludes the elevation proxy); access-constrained streets hold 2,998 of 27,060 units (11%; was 3,054) and the Leave Early Zones 7,736 (R0 bars 18,334 people, was 18,332). **R3 (fire off, study zones, median of 3 seeds, maximum build-out vs none):** fall weekday S4 90th percentile R02 1:35 → 2:15 (+42%; was +30%; study +58%), R04 1:47 → 3:50 (+115%; was +2%; study +25%), R05 1:45 → 1:48 (+3%; study +97%); summer night S3 R02 1:32 → 1:45 (+14%; study +123%), R04 1:34 → 2:42 (+72%; study +52%), R05 1:32 → 1:35 (+3%; study +177%). 100th percentile S4: R02 3:25 → 4:39, R04 3:01 → 4:23, R05 3:29 → 3:29. Order-zone vehicles R02 25,183 → 45,002, R04 1,208 → 2,869, R05 17,086 → 46,938. Gridlock relief (seed 1) with maximum growth: R04 707–955 per case (0 without growth), R02 751–2,137 (0–48 without). `tests/stuck.mjs` with maximum growth (R04 S4, t+120 min; R02 S4, t+150) shows the queues locked on Panoramic Wy, Dwight Wy and Dwight Pl in BER-013, Arden Rd, Mosswood Rd, Orchard Ln and Prospect St at the hill's one way out, with Oxford St and Center St downtown in R02. Before Z1, about half of Panoramic Hill's cars counted as out on reaching the no-zone Oakland part of the hill (K7: 393 of 695 in S2 without growth), so the earlier +2% measured mostly that gap. Middle housing (870 units) still changes no 90th percentile by more than 1 min. **R4 (fire on, default plan, scenario time of day, seeds 1–5):** maximum growth vs none still raises people still there when embers arrived in all 30 pairs; medians eastWind central 5,097 → 14,568, high 35,158 → 68,720; ref1923 central 6,042 → 27,199, high 44,588 → 76,835; panoramic central 16,036 → 30,562, high 51,979 → 67,686. 90% clearance rises in every pair where it is reached: eastWind +13 min central, +36 high; ref1923 +54 central (was +12, +33, +40). ref1923 high: 90% not reached within 6 h in 4 of 5 seeds with maximum growth (was 3), 120–129 min without growth (was 110–123), vehicles cut off by fire 943–1,220 → 2,511–3,222. Moderate growth adds 446–1,987 people still there at central severity (every pair) and 0–4 min to 90% clearance. **R5:** every R0 area still lowers the count in all 30 pairs, and R0 in Fire Zones 2–3 returns the no-growth result in every pair. Central, difference of medians: Leave Early Zones −1,750 eastWind, −3,111 ref1923, −10,815 panoramic; access-constrained areas (bars 7,105 people) −1,503, −2,102, −5,133. Per person barred, access-constrained R0 still removes more than Leave Early Zones R0 on all three fires (0.21 vs 0.10 eastWind, 0.30 vs 0.17 ref1923, 0.72 vs 0.59 panoramic). 90% clearance where both runs reach it: Leave Early Zones −1 to −17 min, access −1 to −15, lower in every pair in eastWind (both severities) and ref1923 central. With moderate growth, Leave Early Zones R0 lowers the count in every pair (−33 to −1,264). | **conflict, disclosed**: the large regions still absorb growth more easily than the study's network (R3's explanation holds for R02 and R05). Panoramic Hill now does the reverse: with its cars confined to one exit, its streets lock up and the gridlock rule fires hundreds of times per run, so its growth figure rests on the jam rule more than the others and is less certain. Results panel text (`js/main.js`, Assumptions & limits) and README limits updated to these figures. |
| R8 | **R3–R5 rerun after opening The Crossways (K8) and taking BER-013's Census count (Z2), 2026-11-01** (`node tests/growth.mjs` on eastWind, ref1923 and panoramic at central and high severity, seeds 1–5; `GROWTH=max node tests/calibration.mjs`).
