Signs: each of the 589 distinct signs governs its side of the segment it lies within 20 m of, for the whole segment (250 segments, 58 signed on both sides). Daylighting: at each segment end meeting an intersection (3 or more vehicle segments), one parked side loses 20 ft of curb (with two parked sides, each loses 20 ft at a two-corner segment and one side at a one-corner segment; one parked side takes every corner's 20 ft; corrected after Codex review of PR #55, which found a one-side segment losing only 10 ft); this lowers the parked-car count and so the chance of a bad parking job, and leaves 97 short segments (seed 1, day; 94 at night) with no room to park on at least one side; the model has no position-along-the-block, so corners are not widened. Robustness, medium severity, seeds 1–5, today's plan against "both" (medians; B lower/higher in how many of 5): east wind and north hills (their time of day gives the same engine figures), hill residents beyond the 5-minute engine standard 1,541 → 1,187 (5/5 lower), engine-blocked segments 283 → 252 (5/5); 1923 repeat and Panoramic Hill 1,235 → 1,100 (4/5 lower, 1 equal), 211 → 192 (5/5). People still there when embers arrived, fire front, 90% clearance and cars cut off move by under 1% in medians; on the 1923 repeat embers 4,569 → 4,592 (higher in 4/5); the 90% clearance median stays at 105 min (higher in 2/5, lower in 2/5). Rerun after the per-side daylighting fix (Codex review of PR #55); medians unchanged. `tests/plan-draws.mjs` covers the setting (336/336 like for like). | **built**, Expert only; not a Simple-view question (that would need the P19 test and Eric's decision). |
| PH16 | **NIST TN 2205** (Maranghides, Link, Brown, Mell, Hawks, Wilson, Brewer, Vihnanek, Walton, "WUI Fire Hazard Mitigation Methodology", 2022, doi:10.6028/NIST.TN.2205; read 2026-11-04). Table 2 (p. 8): WUI types by structure separation distance (SSD): high density 6–30 ft, medium 30–100 ft, low over 100 ft. p. 28: "High Density (SSD on the order of < 25 ft) – must not have any structures ignite as risk of entire community loss is very high due to structure-to-structure fire spread"; 25–50 ft has the "greatest impact on structure ignition resistance by hardening"; over about 50 ft, "low potential for urban conflagration". pp. 31–32: a "sweet spot" in spacing where hardening adds value; under 25 ft "hardening will have limited or no effect in reducing the ignition potential of the structure". pp. 42–43: in high density "ignition of a structure will almost invariably result in the ignition of one or more adjacent" properties; spread is hard to stop between buildings "6 ft to 10 ft apart"; fn. 11: ignitions from embers from burning homes "as far as 200 ft to 300 ft downwind". | **pending**: bands and where hardening acts for step 3e (`docs/design/structure-spread.md`). |
| PH17 | **NISTIR 8436** (Prasad and Maranghides 2022, doi:10.6028/NIST.IR.8436; read 2026-11-04). Fire-dynamics simulation; with 20 kW/m² as "a rough approximation for ignition flux" of wood, a 16 MW burning structure in 4 m/s wind needs SSD 9.6 m (wall) and 8.3 m (eaves) to stay below it (pp. 13–14). | **context**: upper bound near 10 m for flame and radiant ignition between structures; embers excluded. |
| PH18 | **Knapp, Valachovic, Quarles and Johnson 2021**, "Housing arrangement and vegetation factors associated with single-family home survival in the 2018 Camp Fire", Fire Ecology 17:25, doi:10.1186/s42408-021-00117-0 (read 2026-11-04). Homes built before 1997: 11.5% survived; 1997 and later: 38.5% (abstract). Regression tree (Fig. 5a, p. 9): within 18 m of a destroyed structure, survival 0.058; beyond 18 m, 0.354 (26.3% of homes beyond 18 m). | **pending**: the empirical near-neighbor loss probability for step 3e option A (about 0.94 lost within 18 m). |
| PH19 | **Zamanialaei et al. 2025**, Nature Communications 16:8041, doi:10.1038/s41467-025-63386-2 (about 47,000 structures, Tubbs, Thomas, Camp, Kincade and Glass fires). Separation distance the most important predictor of loss in four of five fires (Figs. 4–5); scenario (Fig. 7): all structures hardened, 25% survival; hardening plus Zone 0, 40%; plus Zones 0–1, 48%. From a research summary (Claude Code subagent, 2026-11-04); not re-read; the no-mitigation baseline was not found in the text read. | **pending**: upper bound for hardening and Zone 0 settings in step 3e; to be read before any number reaches the simulator. |
| PH20 | **The 1991 fire's structure loss rate.** USFA-TR-060 (Routley, *The East Bay Hills Fire*, 1992; Cal OES copy): "Almost 800 structures were ignited within the first hour and more than 300 per hour for the next seven hours" (p. 51); "3,354 single family dwellings and 456" apartment units destroyed (p. 45). NFPA, *The Oakland/Berkeley Hills Fire* (Cal OES copy): "it consumed 790 structures within the first hour, and spread about 1.67 meters per second" (PDF p. 12); "it ignited one building every 11 seconds" (PDF p. 14, OCR "1 1"); 2,449 single-family dwellings and 437 apartment and condominium units (PDF p. 4). Both read 2026-11-04. **Conflict:** the reports' totals differ (3,354 vs 2,449 single-family); one building every 11 s for 10 h is about 3,270, closer to USFA's. | **pending**: calibration target for step 3e option B. |
| PH21 | **Context sources (not re-read).** Syphard et al. 2012, PLoS ONE (doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0033954): loss most likely at low to intermediate structure density in southern California wildfires. Syphard, Brennan and Keeley 2014, IJWF 23:1165 (abstract): clearance most effective at 5–20 m, none added beyond 30 m. Mockrin et al. 2023, J. Env. Mgmt 347:118960 (Woolsey Fire): proximity to destroyed buildings the variable most related to loss. Hamada model (Japanese urban fire spread; original not retrieved): spread rate from building size, separation, wind and fire-resistant share. Troy et al. 2022 and Syphard and Keeley 2019 could not be retrieved. | **context**: a structure rule must not assume loss rises with density everywhere; the Hamada form is the precedent for a separation-keyed cell rule. |
| PH22 | **Step 3e option B scratch test (Eric, 2026-11-04: yes to the test, adopt nothing).** `node tools/structure-spread-test.mjs 3`: the spread factor in the 5,172 Fire Zone 2–3 cells holding City footprints is redistributed by how closely their structures stand ("separation": weights 1 under 25 ft, 0.6 at 25–50 ft, 0.3 beyond; or "coverage": footprint share of the cell), keeping the mean over those cells at the proxy's, ember throw unchanged. 1923 repeat, medium, seeds 1–3, minutes to the 1923 entry point (StoryMap about 150) and to Shattuck (about 300): proxy 104/116/54 and 264/285/220; separation 103/115/53 and 257/281/223; coverage 115/122/56 and 239/278/205 (the 1.5 cap reaches 184 cells; the mean is restored after the cap, Codex review of PR #55; before that fix the coverage variant ran 5.6% slow overall). 1991-like fire (high severity, winds 30/45/60°, seeds 1–3, medians; origin our placement, fire-validation §4b): City-footprint structures reached in 1 h: 38, 36, 37; 2020 Census housing units in 1 h: 492 under every variant (the 1991 area is in Oakland, outside the City footprints, so the variants cannot touch it); at 4 h 559–575 structures and 4,267–4,284 housing units. Record: "Almost 800 structures were ignited within the first hour" (PH20). | **not adopted**. Redistributing the factor by building spacing moves the 1923 marks by up to 25 min and does not fix the early entry, so it buys little on its own. The 1991 check cannot test it without footprints in Oakland; with Census housing units standing in, the model fire reaches about 530 units in its first hour against about 800 structures, while running 1.6–1.8 times too far downwind in its first hour (FV9): it runs fast and narrow. Next for option B, if pursued: building footprints outside Berkeley (Microsoft or Overture), then a hardening setting that acts through the spread factor. |
| PH23 | **Step 3e option A built (Eric, 2026-11-04).** Results row "Structures lost where the fire front reached them (Fire Zones 2–3), same under every plan" (Expert and Simple views' results panel, labeled assumption): for each cell the fire front has reached, structures under 50 ft from the nearest one × 0.85–0.94, those 50 ft or more apart × 0.27–0.65 (`data/assumptions.json` `structures.lossIfFront`; Knapp et al. 2021, PH18: survival 0.058 within 18 m of a destroyed structure; beyond 18 m 0.354–0.733 by branch; the 0.85 lower end is our allowance; Knapp's 18 m is 59 ft, so structures 50–59 ft apart take the beyond-18 m range). Embers alone: no loss figure. The fire does not respond to structures. End of run, medium severity, seeds 1–3: 1923 repeat 1,429 reached, 1,195–1,333 lost (the footprint clip makes all seeds equal); east wind 553–575 reached, 458–535 lost; Panoramic Hill 257–258, 212–239; north hills 1,430–1,616, 1,204–1,513. High severity: east wind 1,233–1,468 reached, 1,031–1,370 lost; north hills 2,993–3,541, 2,522–3,315. Equal under today's plan and a leave-early-plus-enforcement plan for every fire and seed. | **built**. Hardening and Zone 0 settings wait (Eric, 2026-11-04): with the fire fixed, a setting could act only on losses at 25–50 ft (496 structures) and would understate hardening, whose value in dense blocks is preventing the first ignition (NIST TN 2205 p. 28). |

| PH26 | **Berkeley's fire zones were redefined in 2025, and the City's GIS still shows the 2013 map.** Special Item 02, "Modification and Adoption of Berkeley Fire Code", Council 2025-04-15 (63 pp.; PDF p. 3 and Attachment 1, PDF pp. 34–35): Fire Zones 1, 2 and 3 are renamed the Flatlands (Zone 1), Hills (Zone 2) and Panoramic (Zone 3) Mitigation Zones, and a Grizzly Peak Mitigation Zone (Zone 4) is created, "the area east of Grizzly Peak Boulevard to the city boundary west, north and south", with homes fronting either side of Grizzly Peak Blvd included; the Panoramic zone is "bounded by the line formed by" Canyon Rd, Stadium Rim Way, Bancroft Way and Prospect St; the Hills zone is the Very High and High zones outside Zones 3 and 4, east of a line of named roads (Arlington Ave, Sutter St, Spruce St, Gayley Rd, Piedmont Ave, Warring St, Belrose Ave, Claremont Blvd, Claremont Ave). Every City fire-zone layer (`FireZones_WFL1`, `FireZonesBerkeley_WFL1`, `HFZ_shp`, the Genasys copies; edited 2019–2024) still shows Zones 1–3 of the 2013 map, with Zone 3 one polygon of 0.12 km² (PH14). The City layer `grizz_pano_mitigation` (edited 2026-03-13) has the two new areas: one of 72 acres measured in UTM (324 cells of 30 m: 134 of the model's 141 Fire Zone 3 cells plus 188 Fire Zone 2 cells), on Panoramic Hill, and one of 271 acres (1,231 cells, all in the model's Fire Zone 2), east of Grizzly Peak Blvd. That layer's `area_acre` field gives 136.2 and 451.8 acres and its `Shape__Area` 115.7 and 436.3 acres (ISSUES.md I3). | **open**: the model's proxy fuel uses the 2013 zones, so today's Panoramic zone is 2.3 times the model's Fire Zone 3 and the Grizzly Peak zone is new. Re-keying the proxy is a model change for Eric; it bears on the LANDFIRE decision only through NB1 cells, which keep the proxy. |
| PH27 | **LANDFIRE canopy over its burnable cells** (`node tools/canopy-check.mjs`; LF2025 CBH and CBD on the fire grid, LANDFIRE encodings meters × 10 and kg/m³ × 100). Screen for crown-fire potential (our rule for this check): canopy base height ≤ 2 m and bulk density ≥ 0.10 kg/m³. Timber litter (TL), 13,424 cells, all with canopy (median base height 2 m, density 0.09 kg/m³): 2,350 pass (17.5%), by model TL6 1,723 of 4,107 (LANDFIRE-setting spread factor 0.42), TL3 360 of 5,277 (0.12), TL8 236 of 412 (0.43), TL5 22 of 3,160 (0.32), TL9 8 of 9 (0.64), TL4 1 of 328 (0.17). Timber understory (TU), 1,875 cells: 600 pass (32%), TU2 566 of 1,415 (factor 0.86), TU5 27 of 312 (0.65), TU3 7 of 148 (1.21). Grass-shrub 22 of 6,752; grass 0 of 3,312; shrub 3 of 1,692. Corrected 2026-11-04 after Codex review of PR #56, which found the first version counted the understory cells as slow litter. | **context for PH13**: 2,350 timber-litter cells, mostly TL6 and TL8 at a factor of about 0.42 and TL3 at 0.12, spread under the LANDFIRE setting as slow surface litter although their canopy could carry crown fire, consistent with 1991's crowning eucalyptus and pine (fire-validation section 1, Limits). The setting likely understates spread there. The 600 understory cells already spread at 0.65–1.21. A crown-fire factor would need a sourced rule. |
| PH28 | **LANDFIRE default-fuel test (Eric, 2026-11-04: "Outlines then switch"), and the decision.** `node tools/mitigations.mjs --fuel landfire --workers 4`: every lever and its baseline on the LANDFIRE fuel grid, fires eastWind, ref1923, panoramic, northHills, low/central/high × seeds 1–5 (1,040 runs; 980 of 980 like for like; output `data/model/mitigations-landfire.json`, `docs/mitigations-landfire.md`). Against the default run (`data/model/mitigations.json`), 54 lever-fire results keep the same set of measures that hold in every pair; 14 measures lose it and 1 gains it (order sooner, embers, east wind: 14/15 → 15/15). The losses: leave early (north hills embers and fire front; Panoramic Hill cars cut off and fire front; 1923 repeat cars cut off and fire front), order sooner (north hills embers; Panoramic Hill cars cut off and fire front; 1923 repeat fire front), the Panoramic Hill second road (cars cut off), and the Panoramic Hill and top-100 buyouts (cars cut off; top-100 also fire front). 13 of the 14 lose it to ties only: every pair that is not better is a low-severity run in which the slower fire reaches almost no one, the same in both plans (for example 1 car cut off in both, or 2.37 people, one synthetic household, in both). One pair goes the other way: leave early, Panoramic Hill, low severity, seed 3, people still home when the fire front arrived 2.37 → 4.74 (one household). No answer changes direction. **Decision (Eric, 2026-11-04): keep the fire-zone proxy as the default (option a).** Reasons: LANDFIRE fits the 1923 timing better (FV5), but it slows 2,350 timber-litter cells whose canopy could carry crown fire (PH27), the proxy that its built cells keep still uses the 2013 fire zones (PH26), the overshoot after the 1991 fire's first hour is there under either fuel (PH25), and switching would withdraw 14 Simple-view claims on ties. | **decided**: default unchanged; revisit after a sourced crown-fire rule for those stands and the 2025 zones. |

### Calibration check (fire off, study order/warning zones, seed 1), 2026-10-31

90th / 100th percentile ETE, h:mm after the order. Run with `node tests/calibration.mjs`.

| Region · case | Study p90 | Simulator p90 | Study p100 | Simulator p100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R02 1923 · S1 summer midweek | 2:25 | 1:44 | 3:40 | 3:31 |
| R02 1923 · S2/S3/S5/S6 (weekend, night) | 1:45–1:50 | 1:33–1:34 | 3:25 | 3:05 |
| R02 1923 · S4 fall midweek | 2:55 | 1:50 | 4:10 | 3:31 |
| R04 Panoramic · S2/S3/S5/S6 | 1:35–1:40 | 1:33 | 3:25 | 2:45 |
| R04 Panoramic · S1/S4 | 1:40–1:45 | 1:46–1:53 | 3:40 | 3:15 |

Reading: night and weekend cases are within about 15 min at the 90th percentile; the study says these are set by mobilization, which we now take from the study. Weekday daytime cases are too fast because employees, students, visitors and through traffic (E12) are missing; the study says congestion sets those cases. The 100th percentile is 20–40 min short, partly because the study's tail includes 180-min transit-dependent mobilization (E29), which we don't model.

**Recheck 2026-11-01** after the parking, take-turns and returning-commuter changes (median of 3 seeds): R02 S2/S3/S5/S6 1:34–1:35, S1 1:45, S4 1:51; R04 S2/S3/S5/S6 1:35, S1 1:45, S4 1:51 (range up to 2:09). All vehicles now finish in every case (100th percentile R02 3:09–3:31). These changes move the 90th percentile by about 1–2 min: evacuation traffic runs mostly one way, so taking turns rarely binds, and departure timing and intersections still set the ETE. Engine access is where parking matters: hill length closed to engines is about 10–12 km under the status quo, 6–9 km with tight-to-curb parking, 3–4 km with one side only, 5–6 km with no parking on City narrow roads.

**Recheck 2026-11-01 after the zone fix (W2):** R02 evacuating vehicles fell from about 25,500 to 20,300 (order zones 27,097 people → 11,390 households → 12,714 vehicles; warning zones add about 7,600). ETE barely moved: R02 S2/S3/S5/S6 1:34–1:35, S1 1:46, S4 1:52; R04 S2/S3/S5/S6 1:32, S1 1:48, S4 1:50. The Time Study cites about 27,830 evacuating vehicles for R02 (E13), which likely includes demand we don't model yet (employees, students, visitors); the earlier, closer vehicle count was partly the bug.

**Recheck 2026-11-01 with non-resident demand, the study's ETE definition (E37) and the jam fixes** (median of 3 seeds, untuned):

| Region · case | Study p90 | Simulator p90 |
|---|---|---|
| R04 Panoramic · S1–S6 | 1:35–1:45 | 1:32–1:44 |
| R02 1923 · S2/S3/S5/S6 | 1:45–1:50 | 1:29–1:31 |
| R02 1923 · S1 / S4 | 2:25 / 2:55 | 1:34 / 1:36 |
| R05 Fire Zones 2&3 · S2/S3/S5/S6 | 1:45–1:55 | 1:31–1:32 |
| R05 · S1 / S4 | 2:05 / 2:20 | 1:40 / 1:44 |

Every vehicle now finishes; gridlock relief fires 0–26 times per run (was thousands while jams were being found). Jams fixed on the way: Lab employees and students had been placed on Panoramic Hill; voluntary evacuees drove up Panoramic Hill to reach the Oakland line; dead ends where the County file stops (Tunnel Rd, MLK) had no exit; the take-turns rule starved one direction; a fixed processing order starved junction approaches; U-turn rules trapped cars in dead-end loops and sent them back and forth around Orchard Ln / Arden Rd; capacities under one car per 5 s step were rounded down.

**Tuning attempt (one setting, intersection capacity scale), tuned on R02 S3 and S4, checked on the rest:** at 0.4 the R02 tuning cases match (S3 1:38, S4 2:54) and the held-out R02 cases improve, but Panoramic Hill becomes 25–70 min too slow, R05 barely changes, and R02 S4's last vehicle leaves at 5:45 (study 4:10) with about 4,500 gridlock reliefs. Scaling only arterial and collector junctions barely moves anything (S4 1:49 at 0.2). **Conclusion:** uniform capacity changes trade one region against another, and the remaining gap most likely comes from what the simulator does not model (real signal timing and phasing, where employees and students actually park and which streets they use, through-traffic routes). Left untuned at 1. KLD's network files or the City's signal timing would address it.

**After diverters, circles and night parking (2026-11-01, 3 seeds):** within 1–2 min of the table above in every case (R02 S3 1:32, S4 1:35; R04 S3 1:32, S4 1:45; R05 S3 1:32, S4 1:45). Every vehicle finishes.

The diverters move every 90th percentile by at most 2 min. Panoramic Hill routing shifts the way the Time Study describes (E24, F-4): Derby St traffic heading west now turns south on Piedmont Av at the diagonal diverter instead of crossing it. In R04 S2 (seed 1), order-zone vehicles that used Piedmont Av rose from 49 to 251 and Derby St from 117 to 211; Claremont Blvd fell from 170 to 89 and Claremont Av stayed near 140 (153 → 138). Reopening Derby St between Warring St and South St (O2a) gives Panoramic Hill a Derby St route again, which is why Derby St carries more, not less. The OKL-003 override adds about 140 order-zone vehicles to R04; its weekday 90th percentile rises 3–4 min (S4 1:44, study 1:45) and its S4 100th percentile from 3:17 to 3:26 (study 3:40). Every vehicle finishes in every case; gridlock relief fires 0–40 times per run (R02 S4 the most). Engine travel times in `tests/headless.mjs` are unchanged. The calibration printout no longer shows a rounded 60 seconds as "2:60".

Also consistent with the study: in the 1923 repeat, 50% leave-early (Plan B) changes the 90th-percentile ETE by about 1 min. The study found −3% to −14% with normal mobilization (E13). It does cut exposure to fire and embers by about 40% in the simulator.

## Access-constrained areas: choke points on the drivable network, 2026-11-01

Step 1 of the egress and managed-retreat work. `tools/access-constraints.mjs` finds every street link whose loss leaves homes with no drivable route out of the model area, on the residents' network exactly as `js/sim.js` builds it (barrier-closed segments removed, one-way streets one way, exits where streets leave the elevation grid), and describes the area behind each. Results: `data/model/access-constraints.json`; report with method, tables and checks: `docs/access-constraints.md`. Residents are the simulator's synthetic allocation (expected values); all numbers below are model results, not predictions.

| # | Insight | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | 5,734 of the model area's 41,395 hill residents (14%) live behind at least one choke point; in Berkeley and LBNL zones 3,133 of 27,759 (11%). 4,463 are behind a true dead end, 1,271 behind a choke made only by one-way rules, 683 behind two or more chokes in series, 1,168 behind a choke under 10 ft clear with status-quo parking in its nominal state (every route from their street to every exit crosses the narrow segment; 1,209 before the AC12 correction). 575 areas, 493 with at least one resident. | **derived**: report totals; inputs for steps 2–3 (HANDOFF). |
| AC2 | Panoramic Hill is the largest hill area: 941 residents, 397 households, 444 vehicles on 4.7 km of street, up to 4 chokes in series on upper Panoramic Way. Agrees with SB 99 (p. 4): one access point for the whole neighborhood. | **context**: sanity check passed. |
| AC3 | **Conflict, County vs OSM and SB 99.** The County centerline file codes the 45 m piece from the foot of Panoramic Way to Prospect St as "BANCROFT STEPS WY", TRVLWAY NT (network edge 2322, mode path). OSM maps it as drivable Bancroft Way (way 24025453, tertiary) joining Prospect St, Canyon Rd and Panoramic Way, and SB 99 (p. 4) puts the neighborhood's single access at "Panoramic Way and Prospect St/Canyon Rd". In the model Panoramic Hill therefore drives out over Rim Rd and Stadium Rim Rd around Memorial Stadium. With the piece opened (`--open 2322`) the choke moves to the foot of Panoramic Way (900 residents behind it, 9 ft clear, engine does not fit). | **applied 2026-11-01** as a labeled County correction (K6), not as an edit to the builder's code. |
| AC4 | **Allocation artifact.** Genasys zone ALB-011 ("Albany Hill Wildland Park Area", 2,535 people from 2020 Census blocks) has one drivable segment whose midpoint lies in it (Taft St, 221 m), so the simulator places about 1,070 households on that one dead-end segment. The Census blocks most likely hold the towers at the foot of Albany Hill. | **pending**: place zone populations by Census block or onto streets within a buffer of the zone outline. Flagged ⚠ in the report. |
| AC5 | **Network error.** County edge 2386 (Martin Luther King Jr Wy, 12 m, one-way) and the one-way OSM carriageway (way 272608978) both point into the same node at the Oakland line, so 5.8 synthetic residents placed there can never leave. | **pending**: fix the join in `tools/build-network.mjs` step 3b. |
| AC6 | Zone BER-013 (Genasys, 83 people, "Panoramic Hill") contains no street-segment midpoint, so the simulator places none of its people. | **fixed 2026-11-01** (Z1): BER-013 takes its Genasys outline; its 83 people now sit on Dwight Wy, Dwight Pl and upper Panoramic Wy and Pl in Oakland, all behind the Panoramic Wy choke (AC14). |
| AC7 | SB 99 Table 1 (primary AINs, pp. 6–9; text extraction gives 138 rows and 1,446 parcels of the stated 141 and 1,461): 113 of 132 named-street rows match a choke area with a street of that name in the same or a neighboring zone (1,262 parcels). The 19 misses are mostly flatland blocks that SB 99 counts as dead ends because of diverters (Lincoln St, West St, California St, Delaware St, Mc Gee Ave, 8th St, Colby St), streets the network lacks (Martin Ave, North Valley St, Mystic Ct), and a few hill rows (Eunice St, Ross St, Grizzly Peak Blvd, Fernwald Rd, Claremont Blvd). Ten Berkeley hill dead ends with 5 or more residents have no street in Table 1 (largest: Smyth Ln 54, Bay Tree Ln 43, Summit Rd 34, Somerset Pl 32); SB 99's primary list also requires being outside the 5-minute fire-station service area, which would explain them. Table 1 typos read as Hilgard Ave ("Higard"), North Valley St ("Noth"), Acton Circle ("Action"). | **context**: agreement is good where both methods apply; the differences are explained by SB 99's service-area filter and the model's diverter shapes. |
| AC8 | City Narrow_Roads: only 50 of 326 surveyed segments (4.5 of 40.1 km) lie behind a choke, and 28 of 185 mostly-hill areas have a surveyed narrow road at the entrance. Narrow roads sit mainly in the looped grid north of Codornices Park (SB 99's secondary AINs), dead ends elsewhere; Panoramic Hill is where the two coincide. | **context**. |
| AC9 | Largest one-way areas are in Southside and by the stadium (Bancroft Wy 1,013 residents; Dwight Wy/Dana St 457; Dwight Wy above Piedmont 252; Blake St 249; Piedmont Av/Durant Av 240), from County one-way directions plus diverters. | **context**: softer than dead ends (police or drivers would override one-way rules); tied to HANDOFF item 4b on County one-way directions. |
| AC10 | Edge effect: 75 of 167 exits are on hill streets, none in a Berkeley or LBNL zone (Oakland 39, Kensington/El Cerrito 17, Orinda 11, unincorporated Contra Costa 7, Warren Freeway 1). Courts cut by the grid edge (Highgate Ct, Morrill Ct, Doncaster Pl, Glenwood Glade) count as open, so totals outside Berkeley near the edge are a lower bound. | **context**: noted in the report. |
| AC11 | OSM turnarounds and doubled driveway pieces make small loops (Caldecott Ln) that would add false nesting. Model rule: loops under 200 m of street do not start a nested area. | **applied** in the tool. |
| AC12 | **Corrections from Codex review on PR #3, 2026-11-01.** (1) `--open` left an already drivable one-way segment one-way, so opening a one-way street changed nothing; every opened segment now runs both ways. Opening edge 1190 (Ellsworth St, one-way toward node 650) now takes AC-074 (Bancroft Wy, 1,013 residents) off the list. Codex's repro, edge 379, already runs out of AC-074, so opening it adds only an inbound lane and AC-074 correctly stays. (2) The driving distances behind `drivingNowM` and the 300 m / 3× filter ignored one-way directions. They now follow legal directions from the area point to the choke and on to the outside point: AC-073's link to Dwight Wy goes from 607 m to 1,594 m (Codex's figure), and AC-312's nearest other street changes from Cleveland Av (216 m line) to the other carriageway of Buchanan St (8 m line, 303 m by road). The edge-effect count of hill residents within 500 m of driving to a hill exit is directed too and applies the 500 m limit strictly: 5,431 → 4,624 (65 from direction, 742 from the cutoff, which used to admit nodes one segment past 500 m). A second Codex round found that whole segments were counted when either end was within 500 m, even where the one-way direction leads away from that end (Ostrander Rd: 36.6 residents counted though none can reach a hill exit within 500 m). The count now takes only the part of each segment within 500 m of driving along legal directions: 4,624 → 4,085. (3) Hill residents behind an engine-blocked choke counted every resident of an area whose stem had a narrow segment anywhere, including residents between the entrance and that segment. A resident now counts only if the narrow choke lies on every route from their street to every exit: 1,209 → 1,168. In AC-128 (Panoramic Hill) 900 of 941 residents are behind the 9 ft Panoramic Wy segment (edge 992); about 41 on Stadium Rim Rd, Canyon Rd and Rim Rd are not. Each area now reports `residentsBehindEngineBlockedChoke` and the residents behind its narrowest stem choke. | **applied** in the tool and the report; other totals unchanged. |
| AC13 | **Regenerated 2026-11-01 after the Bancroft Wy link correction (PR #9).** The analysis had been built on the network from before that correction, so it still treated the link as stairs and put Panoramic Hill's entrance on Stadium Rim Rd (Codex review on PRs #2 and #11). On the corrected network: 5,684 of 41,395 hill residents behind any choke (was 5,734); Berkeley and LBNL 3,083 of 27,759 (was 3,133); behind a dead end 4,413 (was 4,463); two or more chokes in series 676 (was 683); behind a choke an engine cannot pass 1,156 (was 1,168); 576 areas (was 575). Panoramic Hill's entrance is now the foot of Panoramic Way (AC-159, 889 residents, 9.1 ft clear, engine does not fit), as AC3 anticipated. Figures in AC1–AC2 are those of the earlier network. | **applied**: `data/model/access-constraints.json` and `docs/access-constraints.md` regenerated. Rule added to HANDOFF: rerun the tool after every network rebuild. |

| AC16 | Rerun 2026-11-01 after opening The Crossways (K8) and taking BER-013's Census count, 122 (Z2). Hill residents behind any choke 5,749 → 5,779 of 41,475 → 41,514; Berkeley and LBNL 3,149 → 3,178 of 27,840 → 27,879; behind a dead end 4,479 → 4,508; two or more chokes in series 758 → 797; behind a choke an engine cannot pass 1,236 → 1,275. Panoramic Hill gains BER-013's 39: AC-155 971.9 → 1,010.9 residents (427 households, 477 vehicles), AC-158 614.2 → 653.2, AC-160 436.7 → 475.7, AC-163 Dwight Pl 8 → 11.8, AC-164 upper Panoramic Wy and Pl 32.6 → 48. The Crossways opens a loop between Overlook Rd and Middlefield Rd: the dead end on Overlook Rd shrinks 9.0 → 5.4 residents and the one on Middlefield Rd 12.8 → 7.3. Narrow-roads segments behind a choke 50 → 48 (4.5 → 4.3 km). Still 576 areas, but ids are assigned in order and shifted for 86 areas (for example AC-075 Bancroft Wy → AC-077, AC-058 Wilson Cir → AC-060, AC-073 Regent St → AC-075); AC-155 to AC-164 and AC-315 keep their ids. | **derived**: `data/model/access-constraints.json` and `docs/access-constraints.md` regenerated; the report names BER-013's Census count and The Crossways. |

The constraint ranking (residents × nesting × SB 99 narrowness factor 4/3/2/1 × (1 + hill share)) is a model rule with no calibration; the report shows each component. No simulator code or data changed, so `tests/calibration.mjs` was not rerun; `tests/headless.mjs` runs as before.

## Widening hill streets against parking changes at the same pinch points, 2026-11-01

Step 2b of the egress plan. `tools/widening.mjs` finds hill segments where width binds and screens what widening them would take; `tools/widening-sim.mjs` runs the simulator with each change applied in memory (`data/model/network.json` unchanged); `node tools/widening-all.mjs` reruns everything and writes `docs/widening.md`. Results: `data/model/widening.json`, runs in `data/model/widening-sim.json`. Synthetic households and a screening estimate, not a prediction, engineering or legal analysis. Counts only: parcel and building geometry is fetched, counted in memory and dropped; no parcel identifiers, owners, addresses or imagery are stored.
## Managed retreat: voluntary buyouts by block, 2026-11-01
Step 3 of the egress and managed-retreat plan. `node tools/retreat-sim.mjs` (about 20 minutes on 7 workers) writes `data/model/retreat.json` and `docs/managed-retreat.md`. Residents are synthetic and the fires illustrative; every count below is a model result on synthetic people, not a prediction and not a casualty count. Costs are a screening estimate, not an appraisal, policy or legal analysis. No parcels, owners or addresses are used; web requests carry the generic User-Agent `berkeley-wildfire-simulator/0.1`.

| # | Insight (source) | Status / use |
|---|---|---|
| WD1 | **Source:** Florida DOT Cost Per Mile Models, U19 "Add 2 Lanes to Existing 2 Lane Undivided Arterial (1 Lane Each Side), with 4' Bike Lanes" (October 2024 update, Wayback copy of 2025-10-06, retrieved 2026-11-01): $9,540,676.51 per mile including curb and gutter, sidewalk, drainage, lighting, traffic control and mobilization; its pay items show 14,467.2 SY of new base per mile, 12,096 m² or 7.5 m of added width. Derived: $789 per m² of added pavement, flat terrain, no right of way. Step 2 used the same page's U01 for new roads. | **applied as an assumption**: low pavement cost; high = 2× (a small hillside job in the Bay Area). |

The share is the least supported input for strips 1–11 ft deep. |

With nominal parking 1,268 of 27,835 Berkeley hill residents are beyond 5-minute engine reach; with no parked cars, none. | **derived**: `docs/widening.md`, ranking. |

Eric's tape measure on that block (HANDOFF item 5) would test the 17 ft City width that this rests on. |

Oak Ridge Rd (396 m, 17 ft): 47 → 0 in 15 of 15, either change. ## Secondary egress candidates: where a second way out could go, 2026-11-01
Step 2 of the egress and managed-retreat work. `tools/egress-candidates.mjs` generates and screens candidate links for the dead-end areas of step 1, reruns the choke-point analysis with each (`tools/access-constraints.mjs --links`), and prices them; `tools/egress-sim.mjs` runs the simulator with and without the top links; `node tools/egress-all.mjs` reruns everything. Results: `data/model/egress-candidates.json`, `data/model/egress-sim.json`; report: `docs/egress-candidates.md`. Every web request used the User-Agent `berkeley-wildfire-simulator/0.1`. No simulator code (`js/`), network builder or `data/assumptions.json` changed; the links are added at run time.
| RT1 | **Rule (model rule).** A block is one street segment on which `js/sim.js` places households, in Fire Zones 2–3 or, outside Berkeley, the 90 m hill proxy: 1,803 blocks, 17,500 expected households; blocks with at least 1 expected household are ranked. Over 60 fire runs (all four scenarios in `js/scenarios.js`, low, central and high severity, seeds 1–5, default plan, the scenario's time of day) each block gets the share of runs with a household still home when the fire front arrived (front) and when embers arrived (ember), and with a car on a street when the fire front reached it or with no route at the end (cut); plus no engine within 5 minutes at ignition under status-quo parking (engine, `engineReachTimes`), dead-end chokes in series (nest, `data/model/access-constraints.json`, one-way chokes left out) and the hours of choke capacity the cars behind its outermost choke need, capped at 1 (choke). Score = 3 front + 1 ember + 2 cut + 1 engine + 0.5 nest + 1 choke, with no calibration. | **applied**: `tools/retreat-sim.mjs`; components shown in the report so a reader can re-sort. |
| RT2 | **Ranking.** The top 22 blocks are all behind the foot of Panoramic Way (AC-155); the first elsewhere is Buena Vista Wy (Del Mar Av to its dead end, BER-032) at rank 23. Panoramic Hill blocks score on every static component (up to 4 chokes in series, no engine within 5 minutes in 80% of runs, 31 minutes of choke capacity for the 458 cars behind the 9 ft entrance) while only one of the four fires reaches them (front at most 25%). By fire exposure alone (front, ember, cut) the top blocks are on Buena Vista Wy, Grizzly Peak Bl, Summit Rd, Cedar St and Campus Dr in BER-031, -032 and -040 (front 35–48%). | **context**: both orders are in the report; the choice of weights decides whether Panoramic Hill or the north-east hills lead. |
| RT3 | **Removal without changing other draws.** `world.retreat` in `js/sim.js` (harness setting, no UI): households on bought-out blocks are placed and take every random draw as before, then make no trip, give no ride and are not counted; `clearParking` also takes the parked cars off those blocks. `node tests/retreat-draws.mjs`: 18 of 18 comparisons (three fires, seeds 1–3, AC-155 and every 5th hill block) keep every other household's street, vehicles, kind, leave-early flag, commuter, departure and ride, every non-resident car and the shared random stream identical. With no buyout, `tests/headless.mjs`, `tests/plan-draws.mjs` (180 of 180) and `tests/calibration.mjs` print exactly what main prints (diffed); calibration did not change. | **applied**: `js/sim.js` (constructor, `buildHouseholds`, `summary`, `engineReachStats`, `logOrders`, `logStart`). |
| RT4 | **Buyout cases.** Top-ranked blocks to about 50, 100, 250 and 500 households (73, 107, 251 and 520 expected households on 4, 5, 21 and 43 blocks); Panoramic Hill's upper streets (AC-158, 259 households, 614 residents); everything behind the foot of Panoramic Way (AC-155, 410 households, 972 residents). Only the 500 case leaves Panoramic Hill (Buena Vista Wy, Summit Ln, Summit Rd, Ajax Pl, Grizzly Peak Bl, Hill Rd, Olympus Av). Each case ran with and without the buyout on all four fires, 15 same-seed pairs each (420 runs). | **applied**: report tables. |
| RT5 | **The benefit is the people bought out.** On the Panoramic Hill fire, people still home when the fire front arrived fall by exactly the number of bought-out residents still home in the run without the buyout, in every case (central medians 135, 204, 346, 389, 336, 389), and everyone else changes by at most 1 person in any pair. The neighbors who stay behind the same choke are still home at the fire front in identical numbers in every pair, since departure times do not depend on the streets. Their cars cut off and their 90% out time fall in every pair in none of the 20 case-fire combinations with neighbors left; on the 1923 fire, for example, the upper-streets case moves the neighbors' 90% time 119 → 110 min at central severity but is better in 5, worse in 5 of 15 pairs. Effects better in all 15 pairs: on the Panoramic Hill fire, every case lowers people still there at the fire front and under embers and cars cut off in the whole model area (AC-155 buyout: cars cut off 242 → 94); on the 1923 and both east-wind fires, only the 500-household case does (1923: people still home at the fire front 384 → 325). | **context**: the report separates benefit 1 (bought out) from benefit 2 (neighbors) per pair. Same finding as step 2 (EG rows, PR #15): the streets do not get people out of the house sooner. |
| RT6 | **Engine reach turns on the lowest block of Panoramic Way.** Buyouts take most hill residents no engine reaches within 5 minutes off the count (1,260 → 416 with AC-155 bought out, median of 10 parking draws) because those residents are the ones bought out; no remaining neighbor gains. Buying out only that 49 m block (8.4 households) changes nothing while its parked cars stay (953 above it beyond 5 minutes), and with them gone 953 → 9 (better in 8 of 10; in the other two draws the block's parked cars leave room for an engine, as in seed 4 in ER3). The same comes from a parking rule on that block without a buyout. | **context**: reported as a sensitivity (`clearParking`); supports ER3's field-check request. |
| RT7 | **Acquisition.** ACS 2020–2024 5-year B25077 median value of owner-occupied homes by block group (Census Reporter API, retrieved 2026-11-01; block groups from TIGERweb): the 7 block groups the cases touch have medians $1,031,700–$1,734,400, one is top-coded at $2,000,001 and one has no median. Range = median ± margin of error. B25003 (tenure) requests to Census Reporter timed out on 2026-11-01, and the Census Bureau API needs a key, so renters and owners are not split. | **applied** as assumption: `tools/retreat-costs.mjs`; cache `data/raw/census/acs-retreat.json` (block-group values and the block group of each bought-out segment; no parcel data). |
| RT8 | **Relocation.** 49 CFR 24.101(b): in a voluntary acquisition the relocation provisions apply to tenants who must move, not to the owner-occupant who sells. 24.401(b): homeowner replacement housing payment "may not exceed $41,200". 24.402(b): tenant rental assistance "not to exceed $9,570" (Cornell LII, read 2026-11-01). FHWA Fixed Residential Moving Cost Schedule (Federal Register, July 27, 2021, FR Doc. 2021-15930), California, occupant owns furniture: 4 rooms $1,475, 8 rooms $2,690. Range per household $0 (every household an owner-occupant selling voluntarily) to $43,890 (every household paid the homeowner maximum and an 8-room move). | **applied** as assumption. |
| RT9 | **Demolition and site restoration.** Delaware County (PA) Office of Housing and Community Development, Demolition Program guidelines (file dated 2025-08): "Residential demolition and site restoration costs range from $20,000 to $50,000." Used at $20,000–$100,000 per household, the high end doubled for Bay Area costs and hillside access (assumption). No Bay Area or FEMA per-unit figure was found that separates demolition from acquisition. | **applied** as assumption. |
| RT10 | **Costs and losses.** Screening totals: top 50 $63M–$202M (73 homes, 173 residents displaced); top 100 $93M–$298M (107, 255); top 250 $258M–$673M (251, 595); top 500 $496M–$1.30B (520, 1,231); Panoramic upper streets $265M–$696M (259, 614); all behind the foot of Panoramic Way $395M–$1.11B (410, 972); about $0.9M–$2.8M per household, acquisition almost all of it. Homes and residents are synthetic counts. | **applied**: report. |
| RT11 | **Against step 2 (provisional, PR #15 at commit a7cb54f).** Step 2's top link, AC-160-N3 (Panoramic Wy → Smyth Ln, 88 m), frees 823 hill residents from behind every choke for $1.5M–$6.0M with one building in its corridor, and lowers Panoramic Hill's cars cut off 163 → 150 (CITY-1: 131), with 389 people still home at the fire front either way. Buying out AC-155 removes those 389 and leaves no one behind the choke, at $395M–$1.11B and 410 homes; the upper-streets buyout removes 337 of them at $265M–$696M. | **context**: comparison in the report, marked provisional; recompute after PR #15 merges. |
| RT12 | **Rebuilt 2026-11-03 on the current model** (`node tools/retreat-sim.mjs`; now 75 fire runs, the north hills fire added; ACS block-group values retrieved again). The first block outside Panoramic Hill is Summit Ln (dead end – Summit Rd, BER-031) at rank 30 (was Summit Rd at rank 24). Benefit is still the households bought out: everyone else changes by at most 3 people in any pair (was 1). Everything behind the foot of Panoramic Way: 427 households, 408 people still home at the fire front removed (central median), cars cut off 265 → 93, $428M–$1,149M; upper streets: 276 households, $298M–$731M (unchanged). The ~500-household case now reaches Summit Ln, Summit Rd, Campus Dr, Grizzly Peak Blvd, Ajax Pl, Wilson Cir, Hilgard Ave and Olympus Ave instead of Buena Vista Way, Hill Rd and E Parnassus Ct. The ~100-household case adds one block of Panoramic Wy (edge 6223, 457 m): 8 blocks, about 129 households, $137M–$343M (was 7 blocks, 118 households, $114M–$319M). | **applied**: `docs/managed-retreat.md`, `data/model/retreat.json`. Eric, 2026-11-03: update the Simple-view buyout to the 8 blocks, so the mitigation table and Set a goal's combinations with it were rebuilt (MS, GS7). |
| RT12 | Rerun 2026-11-01 on the final network (The Crossways open, BER-013 at its Census count), with the step 2 comparison now read from data/model/egress-candidates.json on main (EG14) rather than the PR #15 branch. Everything behind the foot of Panoramic Way: 427 households, 1,011 residents; the people the run without a buyout left at home when the fire front arrived, 408 central median, are removed; neighbors behind the same choke still gain in no measure in every pair. Costs per household about $1.0M–$2.7M. Step 2's top link frees 843 hill residents for $1.5M–$6.0M. Unnamed block ends now read "mid-street break" in the report. | **applied**: data/model/retreat.json and docs/managed-retreat.md regenerated. |

| EG1 | City layer `Imagined_Road_Panoramic_Hill_Berkeley_WFL1`, layer 0 "2nd Route Out" (service description "For Evacuation Time Study"; edited 2025-06-27; retrieved 2026-11-01): one line, "Imagined Road", "Connects Dwight Way in Berkeley and Dwight Place in Oakland", 163 m (Shape__Length 206 in Web Mercator units). Normal mobilization: one or two ways out, R04 90th percentile 1:20/1:15/1:20/1:30/1:20/1:30 and 100th 3:40/3:25/3:25/3:40/3:25/3:25 (S1–S6), identical. | EG3 | Grade limits. State Minimum Fire Safe Regulations, 14 CCR §1273.03 (Justia copy, read 2026-11-01): road and driveway grades not to exceed 16% at any point; up to 20% with local approval and mitigation. Sonoma County fire-access bulletin (permitsonoma.org, read 2026-11-01): 15% default, up to 20% with Fire Chief approval. Whether either binds a Berkeley street was not established, and a Berkeley standard was not found. | **applied** as screening flags at 15% and 20% (model rule), not as a legal test. |
| EG4 | Florida DOT Cost Per Mile Models, U01 "New Construction 2 Lane Undivided Urban Arterial with 4' Bike Lanes": $9,116,872.25 per mile, about $5,665 per meter (Wayback copy of 2025-10-06; the live page returned 404 on 2026-11-01). Flat terrain, Florida prices, no right of way. No public California or hillside per-meter figure was found. | **applied** as the low construction cost; the high end is 3 times it for hillside walls, grading and drainage (**assumption**), applied to the length a 15% road would need. |
| EG5 | Civil Design Studio for the Cambria CSD, "Cambria Emergency Evacuation Route: Preliminary Cost Estimate", 2024-02-12 (cambriacsd.specialdistrict.org): about $2.55M with 10% contingency for 12,000 ft (2.25 miles) of 16 ft gravel emergency road on an existing graded alignment, about $697 per meter. | **applied** as the low cost for upgrading a mapped fire road or track (no tested candidate qualified). |
| EG6 | Census ACS 2020–2024 5-year, B25077 median value of owner-occupied homes by block group, read through the Census Reporter API (api.censusreporter.org, 2026-11-01): the Census Bureau API now redirects keyless requests to a "missing key" page. Margins are wide (block group 06001 4227 001 by Panoramic Hill: $1,734,400 ± $891,756) and medians top-code at $2,000,001 in several hill block groups. Block groups from TIGERweb (Tracts_Blocks layer 1) at each link's midpoint, else its ends. | **applied** as the acquisition value per building (median ± margin; **assumption**); partial takes at 10–25% of it per other parcel (**assumption**, no source). |
| EG7 | Alameda County Parcels FeatureServer layer 0 (services5.arcgis.com/ROBnTHSNjoZ2Wm1P, last edited 2026-11-01) carries APNs, situs and mailing addresses and assessed values. Only geometry was requested (outFields OBJECTID), counted per candidate in memory and never written to disk. Contra Costa County (Kensington) is not covered. | **applied**: counts only. |
| EG8 | **Simulator vs Time Study, urgent mobilization (traced 2026-11-01).** Rerun on main's network (Bancroft Wy link open, AC13; BER-013 placed, AC14; Lab gates, AC15). With every household ready within 30 minutes, the simulator clears Panoramic Hill (all of AC-155) to 90% in about 86 minutes on the 1923 fire with one way out (central median, fall weekday midday as in S4), against the study's 1:00 for R04 S4; AC-160-N3 cuts that to 39, AC-134-N2 to 42 and CITY-1 to 54, better in 15 of 15 same-seed pairs each (the study: 0:40). With normal mobilization the links shorten it from 112 to 108–109 minutes (the study: no change at 1:30). Trace, seed 1, central severity: with one way out, Panoramic Hill's 465 resident vehicles queue down Panoramic Way (up to 165 cars) to Rim Rd and Stadium Rim Rd, which feed the queues on Bancroft Wy (about 350 cars) and Piedmont Av; 90% are out at minute 151. With AC-160-N3, 365 of them leave over the link to Smyth Ln and 90% are out at minute 39. **Why the first run differed:** on the network before the Bancroft Wy correction, the area measured (then AC-128) reached up to Stadium Rim Rd, its cars left over the top toward Centennial Dr without queuing (90% out at minute 34 in seed 1), and none of its residents used AC-133-N3 (now AC-160-N3). Instead, 703 vehicles from Smyth Ln, upper Dwight Way and Prospect St drove up the link and through Panoramic Hill to avoid the Dwight Way queue, which slowed the area's own cars (90% at minute 46). That is the simulator's routing on that network, not a harness bug, so nothing in the harness was changed. | **resolved**: on the corrected network the simulator agrees with the study's direction; its gain (about 45 minutes) is larger than the study's (20), and its one-exit time is longer. The trace script was a one-off and is not kept. **Corrections (Codex review on PR #15, rounds 1–4):** the study's Table 10-9 times Panoramic Hill's own vehicles in both regions (§10.4, p. 10-4) in runs with no fire, and its urgent case bounds the whole pre-departure process (commuters included). The benchmark now uses the same orders and time of day (S4) with the fire off, Panoramic Hill's cars, median of 5 seeds: 1923 orders, normal 112 → 108–109 min with a link (study 1:30 → 1:30), urgent 90 → 41–60 (study 1:25 → 1:00; the City's route gives 60); Panoramic Hill orders, normal 118 → 110 (study 1:30 → 1:30), urgent 73 → 37–41 (study 1:00 → 0:40). With urgent mobilization the times with a link match the study; with normal mobilization the simulator is 20–30 min slower than the study and a link changes little, as in the study. Fire on, urgent, Panoramic Hill fire: with one exit the fire cuts off a median 71 of about 479 cars and 90% never get out in most central runs; with a link 90% are out in 44–49 min (better in 10 of 15 pairs; no difference at low severity). |
| EG9 | **Access effect** (rerun 2026-11-01 on main's network). Of 125 links generated for 41 dead-end areas, 78 were pruned (66 for three or more buildings in a 12.2 m corridor, 19 for an average grade over 30%; model rules). A link from upper Panoramic Way to Smyth Ln (AC-160-N3, 88 m), from Smyth Ln to upper Dwight Way (AC-134-N2, 92 m) or the City concept (174 m) leaves 202, 202 and 194 of Panoramic Hill's 972 synthetic residents behind a choke, all on side dead ends (Mosswood Rd, Panoramic Pl, Arden Rd, the top of Panoramic Way, Dwight Pl) whose entrances an engine fits through. Each cuts hill residents behind two or more chokes in series by 695 (of 758) and hill residents behind a choke too narrow for an engine from 1,236 to 264, because all of Panoramic Hill is now behind the 9 ft foot of Panoramic Way. All three links are steep (average grade 21%, 27%, 40%). Smyth Ln is one of the Berkeley hill dead ends missing from SB 99 Table 1 (AC7). Outside Panoramic Hill the largest single gains are Caldecott Ln to Tunnel Rd in Oakland (AC-540-N1, 243) and Oak Ridge Rd (AC-036, 72). First run: 133 generated, 83 pruned; the top links left 167 of 941. | **derived**: `docs/egress-candidates.md` ranked table. |
| EG10 | **Evacuation effect** (rerun 2026-11-01). 900 runs (11 links, 3 fires, 3 severities × seeds 1–5, order at 10 and 20 min, same seed with and without, urgent mobilization for the Panoramic Hill links). A new link does not change how many of an area's synthetic residents are still at home when the fire front arrives (48 of 780 pairs differ, by a household or two). On the Panoramic Hill fire about 163 of Panoramic Hill's ~479 vehicles end on the road with no route (central median); the links cut that to 150 (AC-160-N3), 142 (AC-134-N2) and 131 (CITY-1, better in 15 of 15 pairs), and about 389 synthetic residents are still home when the fire front arrives with or without a link. Buena Vista Wy to W Parnassus Ct (AC-193-N1), 1923 fire: the area's vehicles cut off 4 → 0 (better in 8 of 15, never worse; all vehicles cut off 78 → 70). The first run's Olympus Av path (AC-057-O2: 9 → 2 on the east-wind fire) was not screened this time: the Olympus Av area (now AC-058) has 33.8 residents on main's network (36.3 before) and fell below the top-40 cutoffs (35.6 residents, score 78). First run: 1,380 runs; on the Panoramic Hill fire 129 of ~539 vehicles cut off and a link changed that by 0–2, because Panoramic Hill then drove out over the top. | **derived**: agrees with the Time Study's lesson (E25) for people at home; `docs/egress-candidates.md`. |
| EG11 | The "panoramicExit" variant (County edge 2322 opened, first run: 903 behind the foot of Panoramic Way, top links left 167) is gone: main opened the Bancroft Wy link in the network builder (AC13), so the network as it stands is that variant. Panoramic Hill now has 972 residents behind the foot of Panoramic Way, including BER-013's 83 in Oakland (AC14, AC15). | **applied**: variant removed from `tools/egress-candidates.mjs`, `tools/egress-sim.mjs` and the report. |
| EG12 | OpenStreetMap (Overpass API, overpass-api.de; first run 2026-11-01T17:39Z, rerun 2026-11-01T19:51Z, data as of 19:49Z): building footprints, footways, paths, steps and tracks, parks and protected areas, UC, LBNL and East Bay Regional Park District land within 450 m of each area. Cached in the git-ignored `data/raw/egress/`; only counts and shares are kept in the outputs. | **applied**: buildings in corridor, path share, land flags. |
| EG13 | **Rerun after merging main (2026-11-01).** Merged PRs #2, #3, #9, #11–#14 into the egress branch; `tools/build-network.mjs` rebuilt `data/model/network.json` byte-identical to main's and `data/model/access-constraints.json` identical apart from the build date and an empty `addedLinks` list. Area ids shifted, and candidate ids follow them: AC-133-N3 → AC-160-N3, AC-147-N2 → AC-134-N2, AC-147-N1 → AC-134-N1, AC-128 (Panoramic Hill as measured) → AC-155, AC-541-N1 → AC-540-N1, AC-196-N1 → AC-193-N1, AC-213 → AC-211, AC-226 → AC-225, AC-422 → AC-421, AC-116 → AC-118.
